`wErrLow = emitted_window_min_lufs − TARGET`; `wErrHigh = emitted_window_max_lufs − TARGET`.
The two-sided gate needs `wErrLow ≥ −0.8` **and** `wErrHigh ≤ +0.8`. The whole gate is ±0.5.

**5 of 6 pass the selection. 4D36 fails both halves of it** (`winGate=False`, `wholeGate=False`).
**EF4 passes the window gate by 0.013 LU** — quoted deliberately, because a 0.013 LU margin is
not a pass in any engineering sense; it is a coin-flip that happened to land inside. I am
reporting it as `True` because that is what the gate printed, and flagging it as the second
thing to fix after 4D36.

The whole-program error is **one-sided on all six**: every source lands *below* −16.0 (−0.10 to
−0.70). The limiter's whole-program cost is not being fully repaid by the loop's re-derived
trim, which is itself a finding (§48).

## 47. The failure is one source, and the reason is structural

4D36 is the only source whose **ride demand reaches the +16 dB ceiling**. Sorted
quietest-source-first, to show that source loudness alone does not predict it: E43F and EDU are
nearly as quiet as 4D36 (1.0 and 1.5 LU louder) yet their `c0` peaks land **2.3–2.4 dB lower**.

| src | source I (gated) | ride needed at the worst window | c0 max |
|---|---|---|---|
| **4D36** | **−27.3** | **+13.289 dB** (from §34, round 3) | **15.434** |
| E43F | −26.3 | — | 13.164 |
| EDU | −25.8 | — | 13.041 |
| EF4 | −20.9 | — | 14.057 |
| 56F1 | −20.4 | — | 12.905 |
| 6A58 | −20.3 | — | 12.903 |

(sorted by source I, quietest first; `c0 max` from `iter1_curve.curve_max_db`.) The five passing
sources need at most ~+14 dB of ride and their iteration-2 curve max lands at 13.594–15.214
with **0** points on the +16 ceiling. 4D36 needs +13.289 dB *inside a 240 s window* while its
own c0 already runs at `[9.202, 15.434]`, and its iteration-2 curve reaches and hits +16.

## 48. The curve/chain split after the loop

The loop is a real improvement and it is measurable. This comparison is **aligned-window-family
only for both iterations**, because the two iterations' residuals are stored over *different*
window sets. Iteration 1's `iter1_output_windows` is aligned-only (`cell4.py:353`) and its
stored `iter1_output_residual_max_lu` is the max over that list (`cell4.py:358-360`).
Iteration 2 stores the aligned family in `iter2_output_windows` and the +120 s-offset family
separately in `iter2_output_windows_offset120` (`cell4.py:423-424`), and its both-families
residual lives under a *third* name, `iter3_check.iter2_output_residual_max_lu`
(`cell4.py:410-416`). **There is no stored both-families residual for iteration 1 at all**, so
the two stored numbers are not comparable as-is. Recomputed like-for-like — aligned family
(`iter1_output_windows` vs `iter2_output_windows`):

| src | iter1 max \|err\| (aligned) | iter2 max \|err\| (aligned) | repaid | i1 window min/max | i2 window min/max | i1 limiter cost | i2 limiter cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 0.621 | 0.431 | 0.190 | −16.621 / −16.157 | −16.431 / −16.210 | −1.0 | −1.1 |
| 56F1 | 0.659 | 0.260 | 0.399 | −16.659 / −15.725 | −16.260 / −15.990 | −0.5 | −0.6 |
| **4D36** | **1.137** | **0.992** | **0.145** | −17.137 / −16.302 | −16.992 / −16.472 | −1.4 | −1.4 |
| EDU | 0.310 | 0.094 | 0.216 | −16.286 / −15.690 | −16.094 / −15.956 | −0.3 | −0.4 |
| 6A58 | 0.536 | 0.198 | 0.338 | −16.536 / −15.835 | −16.198 / −16.028 | −0.5 | −0.6 |
| E43F | 0.349 | 0.188 | 0.161 | −16.349 / −15.981 | −16.188 / −16.040 | −0.5 | −0.6 |

**The loop repays 0.145–0.399 LU on every source** — the closed-loop idea works. But:

* **The limiter cost is not repaid at all.** It is −1.4 LU on 4D36 in *both* iterations, and
  −0.5/−0.6, −1.0/−1.1 etc. on the rest: **the iteration-2 number is the same as iteration 1
  to 0.1 LU on every source.** The order's item 3 ("re-measure and re-derive the trim after a
  limit change; a retry must never reuse the stale trim") fixes the *retry* path — but the same
  defect is present in the **loop's own** trim: the iteration-2 render re-derives `trim0`/`trim1`
  from a fresh measurement, yet the residual limiter cost per iteration is unchanged. Whatever
  the loop repays, it is repaying out of the **curve** term, not the chain term.
* **4D36 starts at 1.137 and only repays 0.145.** Compare 56F1, which starts at 0.659 and
  repays 0.399. The repayment is *smallest* exactly where it is needed most.

So after the loop the two terms stand as: **4D36 residual 0.992 LU = ~0.55 LU curve (the clamp)
+ ~1.4 LU chain (limiter, partially offset)**; on the other five the residual is 0.09–0.43 LU.
The clamp is what stops the loop converging on 4D36.

## 49. Root cause 1 — the +16 dB ceiling binds on 4D36, and only there

OBSERVED from `iter1_curve` / `iter2_curve` / `combine_iter2` / `iter3_check`:

| src | c0 [min,max] | c0 hits g_max | c1 [min,max] | c1 hits g_max | sum [min,max] | sum clampLow/High | sum max step dB | corr [min,max] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | [−2.727, 14.057] | 0 | [−4.311, 14.368] | 0 | [−4.311, 14.368] | 0 / 0 | 1.000 | [−1.584, 3.214] |
| 56F1 | [0.197, 12.905] | 0 | [−0.524, 15.214] | 0 | [−0.524, 15.214] | 0 / 0 | 1.000 | [−1.046, 3.426] |
| **4D36** | **[9.202, 15.434]** | **0** | **[8.964, 16.000]** | **54** | **[8.964, 17.296]** | **0 / 54** | 0.942 | [−0.432, 2.197] |
| EDU | [7.660, 13.041] | 0 | [7.105, 14.356] | 0 | [7.105, 14.356] | 0 / 0 | 0.872 | [−0.706, 1.764] |
| 6A58 | [0.200, 12.903] | 0 | [−0.512, 15.193] | 0 | [−0.512, 15.193] | 0 / 0 | 1.000 | [−1.039, 3.422] |
| E43F | [6.948, 13.164] | 0 | [6.895, 13.594] | 0 | [6.895, 13.594] | 0 / 0 | 0.933 | [−0.759, 1.854] |

**Corrected statement of the ceiling finding.** Neither `c0` (4D36 max **15.434**) nor the
correction (4D36 max **2.197**) individually reaches +16. **Only the sum does**: the applied
iteration-2 curve `c1` is clamped at exactly 16.000 on **54 of 1801** grid points, and the
unclamped sum would have reached **17.296**. `clamped_above_g_max: 54` on 4D36, **0 on the
other five**. `clamped_below_g_min` is **0 on all six** — the −6 dB floor never binds, and no
window was held silent (`silent=0`, `hits_g_min=0` everywhere).

Two consequences the coordinator should weigh:

1. **+16 dB is not enough for 4D36, and the shortfall is bounded and small.** The clamp removes
   at most `17.296 − 16.000 = 1.296 dB` at the worst grid point. Raising `G_max` to ~+17.5 dB
   would remove the clamp on this configuration. That is *not* a recommendation — see §57 for
   what that would do to the quiet-gap floor, which already fails on all six (§50).
2. **The sum can move at up to 2× the specified slew rate.** `sum_max_step_db: 1.000` on
   EF4/56F1/6A58, and `sum_max_rate_db_per_s: 1.000` against the specified 0.5 dB/s — where base
   and correction rise together the applied curve moves at 1 dB/s, not 0.5. This is a *design
   fact of the order's recipe as written* (add → clamp → render, no second slew pass), not a
   bug I introduced; `smoke4.py` proves it in isolation (`combine sum rate ok ... sum_max_step_db
   : 1.0, apply_slew: False`). A causal second slew pass over the sum was implemented and
   **rejected on measurement** — `slew_lag_max_db: 2.5` in the synthetic case, and on EF4 the
   loop repaid only 0.09 LU of a 0.62 LU residual with it. It is retained as
   `apply_slew=True` for measurement and is off in every number reported here.

## 50. Root cause 2 — the silence hold does not cover a gap inside a speech-bearing window

OBSERVED from `gap` (`source_gap_mean_m_lufs`, `iter1/iter2/emitted_*`, `emitted_gap_10s_lufs`,
`gap_ends_above_minus35`). `gapSrc` = the source's own quietest 10 s (arithmetic dB mean of M
blocks); `emit_10s` = the same 10 s in the emitted artifact; `lift` = the difference.

| src | gap at (s) | gapSrc LUFS | i1 out | i2 out | emitted mean | emitted 10 s | lift LU | ends above −35 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 406.9 | −35.187 | −34.110 | −33.009 | −33.077 | −32.1 | +3.087 | **True** |
| 56F1 | 1164.3 | −33.849 | −28.993 | −28.785 | −28.848 | −20.4 | **+13.449** | **True** |
| 4D36 | 851.5 | −34.568 | −21.682 | −21.626 | −21.689 | −21.3 | **+13.268** | **True** |
| EDU | 784.6 | *sentinel* | *sentinel* | *sentinel* | *sentinel* | −24.1 | n/a | **True** |
| 6A58 | 1239.4 | −33.849 | −28.965 | −28.755 | −28.813 | −20.4 | **+13.449** | **True** |
| E43F | 147.0 | −50.776 | −39.880 | −38.469 | −38.415 | −21.6 | **+29.176** | **True** |

**All six are flagged `gap_ends_above_minus35: true`.** On 4D36 the room floor rises
−34.568 → −21.3 LUFS (**+13.27 LU**); on 56F1/6A58 **+13.45 LU**; on E43F the emitted 10 s
floor sits at **−21.6** against a source floor of −50.8 (**+29.2 LU**).

**Mechanism (INFERRED from the code, consistent with the measurements):** `gain_curve`'s
silence hold fires only when an *entire* window has no measurable level — i.e. the whole window
is below `SILENCE_FLOOR_LUFS = −70.0` (`envelope.py`). A 10–20 s gap sitting *inside* a 45 s
window that otherwise contains speech makes that window fully measurable, so the window's level
is dominated by the speech and the gap receives the **full ride gain**. The ride is doing
exactly what it was told; the gate definition is what does not see the gap. This is consistent
with `silent=0` on every source in §49 — **the hold never fired once in this run.**

**This is a product question, not an engineering one, and I am not inventing an answer.**
A leveler that lifts the room tone by 13 LU between speakers is not obviously acceptable for a
public-record civic broadcast; but "acceptable" is a policy call (how much room-tone lift is
tolerable in a quiet gap, and whether the hold should be window-relative rather than
absolute-silence-based) and it changes the spec's loudness behaviour. Recorded in
`questions\U25.md` as **Q8**, and recorded there as the *mirror* of round 3's still-open §40
question: §40 found a short pause inside loud speech receiving **≈0 dB** of lift, §50 finds a
10–20 s gap inside a speech-bearing window receiving the **full** ride gain. Both are the same
property of a sliding-window leveler, so a fix has to satisfy both. **Q8 in
`questions\U25.md` should be answered with §40 in view, not §50 alone.**

## 51. The loop does not converge — iteration 3 would move every source

OBSERVED (computed, not rendered — `iter3_check` is a prediction from the same pure `combine`
function applied to the *measured* iteration-2 output series; no third iteration was rendered,
per the order):

| src | iter3 curve delta max | iter3 clamped above g_max | would move a window > 0.1 LU | i2 residual (both families) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 1.9445 dB | 0 | **True** | 0.739 |
| 56F1 | 1.8927 dB | 7 | **True** | 0.260 |
| **4D36** | 1.4847 dB | **97** | **True** | 0.992 |
| EDU | 1.8653 dB | 0 | **True** | 0.119 |
| 6A58 | 1.8889 dB | 8 | **True** | 0.278 |
| E43F | 1.0564 dB | 0 | **True** | 0.194 |

**Every source would move a window by > 0.1 LU at iteration 3**, so the order's "if a third
iteration would change any window by more than 0.1 LU, report it but do not run it" fires on
all six and no third iteration was run. The residual *is* still falling (§48), so the loop is
converging in the limit — but it is nowhere near converged at 2 iterations.

**The clamp is tightening, not relaxing.** On 4D36 the number of ceiling-clamped points goes
**54 (iteration 2) → 97 (predicted iteration 3)**. Note that 56F1 and 6A58, which have
`c1 hits g_max = 0` today, would clamp 7 and 8 points at the next step. That is the signal that
a 2-iteration loop on a +16 dB ceiling is the wrong stopping point for this panel: it stops
*before* the clamp problem arrives on the sources that currently pass.

## 52. The panel is not six recordings — it is four

OBSERVED from `V.SOURCES` (the `src` paths are the live station's upload files; I read the
existing series, I did not re-decode the files for this check):

```
EF4   Parks___Recreation_Advisory_Board_-_April_2026.mp4   in= 7307.947 dur= 446.052  gov
56F1  Parks___Recreation_Advisory_Board_-_April_2026.mp4   in= 3689.216 dur=1800.128  gov
4D36  Sustainability_Advisory_Board_-_April_2026.mp4       in= 2809.997 dur=1800.022  pub
EDU   City_Council_Regular_Session_-_September_8__2026.mp4 in=10219.477 dur=1800.122  edu
6A58  Parks___Recreation_Advisory_Board_-_April_2026.mp4   in= 3614.122 dur=1800.076  gov
E43F  NSF_NCAR_Explorer_Series__The_Day_After_Tomorrow...  in= 1808.405 dur=1800.043  gov
```

**EF4, 56F1 and 6A58 are the same file.** 56F1 (3689.216+1800) and 6A58 (3614.122+1800)
overlap for **1725 of 1800 s (96 %)**. Consequences, all visible in the tables above:

* Their gap triples are identical to three decimals (−33.849 source, +13.449 lift, −20.4
  emitted) because it is literally the same audio.
* Their quietest gap sits at the **same absolute file position** through two different
  `inpoint`s: `3689.216 + 1164.3 = 3614.122 + 1239.4 = 4853.52 s`.
* Their `c0` curves are near-identical ([0.197, 12.905] vs [0.200, 12.903]).

**The "six sources" are four independent recordings** (PARKS, SUSTAIN, CITY9, NSF). Everything
reported here is still honest per-source; the *breadth* of the claim is narrower than the table
implies, and EF4 (the 0.013 LU knife-edge pass) is the same recording as two of the four
"passes". I would not report "5 of 6 pass" to the owner without this caveat attached.

## 53. Timing (order item 5)

All OBSERVED from `timing_s`, which `cell4.py` records per pass. The production chain is 7
decode passes (2 ride-only PCM measures, 2 chain-at-trim0, 2 OUTPUT-at-trim1, 1 encode) plus 3
emitted measurement decodes (AAC, seek-free).

**2.5-hour source (BIG, 9000.0 s, `big-time`, SUSTAIN at inpoint 0):** see §53a below.

**30-minute sources (1800 s, five of them) — the answer is no on both paths:**

| src | dur s | production chain s | × realtime | measurement only s | warm timeout s | fits on-demand 300 s | fits warm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 446.052 | 62.49 | 7.14× | 3.82 | 300.0 | **True** | **True** |
| 56F1 | 1800.128 | 1466.51 | 1.23× | 20.13 | 337.5 | False | False |
| 4D36 | 1800.022 | 1493.57 | 1.21× | 19.64 | 337.5 | False | False |
| EDU | 1800.122 | 1214.24 | 1.48× | 17.55 | 337.5 | False | False |
| 6A58 | 1800.076 | 1187.71 | 1.52× | 14.73 | 337.5 | False | False |
| E43F | 1800.043 | 1877.02 | 0.96× | 21.85 | 337.5 | False | False |

`_warm_timeout_s = max(300, duration/8 × 1.5)`: 300.0 for EF4 (446/8×1.5 = 83.6 → 300), 337.5
for every 1800 s source. **Five of five 30-minute sources miss both the 300 s on-demand
timeout and the 337.5 s warm timeout**, by 3.5×–5.6×. Only the 446 s source fits, and it fits
*both* (62.49 s vs 300 s, 4.8× headroom).

**The box's achieved rate is unstable by up to 15× and I will not extrapolate from it.**
Per-pass wall times from `timing_s`, same source, same work:

| src | p1 ride | p2 chain0 | p3 out@trim1 | p4 ride i2 | p5 chain0 i2 | p6 out@trim1 i2 | p7 encode |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 7.86 | 8.10 | 7.98 | 8.03 | 8.01 | 8.32 | 8.71 |
| 56F1 | 118.96 | 313.84 | 193.96 | 202.15 | 127.13 | 241.41 | 255.54 |
| 4D36 | 144.48 | 308.78 | 207.57 | 170.00 | 201.12 | 214.62 | 236.10 |
| EDU | 197.94 | 150.02 | 129.63 | 131.97 | 277.11 | 184.95 | 132.44 |
| 6A58 | 188.93 | 129.14 | 128.16 | 228.79 | 234.66 | 135.61 | 132.41 |
| E43F | 219.60 | 208.56 | **503.14** | 346.75 | 181.11 | 189.17 | 219.28 |

EF4 does the same work in 7.9–8.7 s **for 446 s of audio** (≈56× realtime per pass).
E43F takes **503 s for one pass over 1800 s** (3.6× realtime) — and its *next* pass takes 181 s.
That is a **15× swing in achieved rate on the same box for the same pass**, and the only
difference is which other tenant was running at that moment. The 1800 s sources ran 2-at-a-time
against four other tenants (below); EF4 ran effectively alone.

So: **the honest answer is that the numbers above are lower bounds measured under contention,
and the per-second rate is not a stable function of anything I can control or predict.** On the
one clean measurement (EF4, ≈56× realtime per pass) a 9000 s source needs 7 × 9000/56 ≈ 1125 s
minimum; on the contended rate it needs ~11000 s. I am not quoting either as a projection.

### 53a. The 2.5-hour timing run — attempted twice, never completed one pass

A dedicated single-source run was launched to give the order a real 9000 s number rather than
an extrapolation:

```
nohup .venv/Scripts/python.exe cell4.py --only NONE --with-big --workers 2 > big.log
```
launched 07:45:48 (python PIDs 1312 / 34340, ffmpeg 10228 spawned 07:45:52), reading
`C:\ProgramData\CivicCast\data\uploads\lpmrot-04-sustaina\Sustainability_Advisory_Board_-_April_2026.mp4`.
The BIG series (9000.0 s, `source_I −26.8`, `LRA 7.7`, `source_TP −0.4`) was cached from an
earlier pass, so the run went straight to **iteration-1 pass A** (the ride-only PCM measure).

| OBSERVED | value | how |
|---|---|---|
| pass-A wall time at kill | **3711 s** (61 min 51 s) | 07:45:52 → 08:47:43 |
| **pass A completed?** | **no — I terminated it** | `taskkill /PID 10228 /F` → `SUCCESS` |
| ffmpeg CPU consumed | 3016.8 s | `Win32_Process.UserModeTime+KernelModeTime` |
| CPU utilisation | 81.3 % of one core | 3016.8 / 3711 |
| source bytes read | 961.8 MB | `Win32_Process.ReadTransferCount` |
| source file size | 1,843,221,952 B = 1843.2 MB | `Path.stat().st_size` |
| **file fraction consumed** | **52.2 %** | 961.8 / 1843.2 |
| read rate | 0.259 MB/s | 961.8 MB / 3711 s |
| per second of source audio | 0.41 s/pass-second | 3711 / 9000 |

So the run was **still working, not hung** (CPU climbing ~56 CPU-s per 60 s wall across three
samples at 08:34:47 / 08:35:47 / 08:36:48: 2410.3 → 2466.9 → 2523.1), and it was **about half
way through the source file** when I stopped it. I stopped it because a single pass had already
run 2.2× the entire warm-timeout budget and the fit question was already answered (§53b); a
full 7-production-pass chain at this rate is ~7 h of box time, which is not proportionate to
finish a question whose answer is already "no".

A *previous* BIG attempt, run concurrently with the six-source run, had reached **1561.8 s of
wall clock without an integrated loudness** and I killed its ffmpeg (`taskkill /PID 28096 /F` →
`SUCCESS`, ~07:40):

```
LOOP BIG srcI=-26.8 i1[trim None->None ...]  ... wall=1561.8s ERR:iteration 1 pass A produced no integrated loudness
```

OBSERVED: that log line, and my ledger entry for the kill. Two consequences: the `ERR` is an
artifact of my kill, not a filter failure; and **that earlier run was not hung either** — the
clean run then went on to exceed 3711 s, so the 1561.8 s attempt was killed ~2.4× too early.
I am reporting that as my error, not as a property of the chain.

**The 2.5-hour source therefore has no full-chain wall time, and I am not inventing one.** What
it has is a hard lower bound: **one of the seven production passes alone took ≥3711 s**, against
a warm budget of **1687.5 s** and an on-demand budget of **300 s**.

### 53b. Does each path fit? — no, on both paths, for both durations

| path | budget | 30-minute sources (measured, full chain) | 2.5-hour source (BIG) |
|---|---|---|---|
| on-demand | **300 s** | **No as measured** — 5 of 5 took 1187.71–1877.02 s (4.0×–6.3× over); see §57, this one is rate-dependent | **No** — one of seven passes ≥3711 s = **12.4× over** |
| whole-asset warm, `max(300, dur/8 × 1.5)` | **337.5 s** (1800 s) / **1687.5 s** (9000 s) | **No as measured** — 5 of 5 over (3.5×–5.6×) | **No** — one of seven passes ≥3711 s = **2.2× over** |
| (446 s source, for contrast) | 300.0 s | **Yes** — 62.49 s, 4.8× headroom | — |

Only the shortest source on the panel (EF4, 446 s) fits either path. **Every 1800 s source
misses both by 3.5×–6.3×, and the 9000 s source misses both by 2.2×–12.4× on a single pass
out of seven.**

### 53c. The per-pass cost is superlinear in timeline length, and I did not isolate why

The strongest contrast is not between sources but between **two decodes of the same file by the
same code**:

| decode of the 9000 s source | wall | × realtime | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `anull,ebur128=peak=true` only (the cached source series) | **88.13 s** | 102× | `BIG.series.json:wall_s` |
| `asetnsamples=n=256:pad=0,asendcmd=f=cmds.iter1.txt,volume` + ebur128 | **≥3711 s** | ≤2.4× | §53a |

**Adding the ride mechanism made the same decode ≥42× slower.** Scaling the *ride* pass
linearly from the measured short and medium sources does not predict this either:

| source | dur | pass 1 (ride only) | × realtime | linear prediction for 9000 s |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 446 s | 7.86 s | 56.7× | 159 s |
| 4D36 (same file as BIG) | 1800 s | 144.48 s | 12.5× | 722 s |
| **BIG** | **9000 s** | **≥3711 s** | **≤2.4×** | — |

So BIG is **5.1× the linear prediction from its own file** and **23× the prediction from EF4**.
Per-second-of-audio cost is 0.018 s/s (EF4) → 0.080 s/s (4D36) → ≥0.41 s/s (BIG).
INFERRED, and I did not test it: the cost is likely in the per-command path
(`asendcmd` re-parsing a 90,000-line, 3.2 MB command file across a longer timeline) or in
demux behaviour over a long non-seeked read from `-ss 0`; the low 0.259 MB/s read rate is also
consistent with disk contention from the three other tenants. **I am flagging this as a risk to
the approach itself, not just to this measurement: if the ride's per-command overhead scales
this way, the `asendcmd` command-file mechanism may not be viable for 2.5-hour assets
regardless of the loudness verdict.** This is a question for the coordinator, not something I
will resolve by guessing — it is recorded in `questions\U25.md`.

## 54. The TP retry (order item 3) — implemented, never exercised

The item-3 fix (re-measure and re-derive the trim after a limit change; never reuse the stale
trim) **is implemented** in `cell4.py`'s `tp_retry` path. **It never fired on any source**:
`tp_retry.triggered: False` on all six, final TPs −1.1 (4D36) to −2.2 (EDU) dBTP against the
−1.0 ceiling. So the fixed code path is **unexercised by this panel** — I cannot report it as
proven, only as present. 4D36 at −1.1 dBTP is the closest to the ceiling and would be the one
to watch if `G_max` is raised (§49 would raise it).

## 56. What I did NOT do, and what I could not verify

* **I did not implement anything.** The order's "if it does not pass" branch is STOP. No
  `preparer.py` change, no `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` bump, no gates, no commits.
* **I did not stage.** Per the order: the coordinator merges U25 + U29 and stages `preparer.py`
  once. I touched **no hunks** in `preparer.py` or `stream/_ffmpeg.py` — the merge list is
  empty from my side.
* **I did not run iteration 3.** The order forbids it when it would move a window > 0.1 LU, and
  it would, on all six. `iter3_check` numbers are computed predictions.
* **I did not run a screen or a setting sweep.** Item 4: one configuration only.
* **I did not exercise the TP retry.** §54. Present, unproven.
* **I could not isolate the chain term between the limiter and the AAC encoder.** §48 measures
  the limiter's cost directly; the remainder is attributable to AAC+emission but I did not run
  the extra encode to split it and will not claim a number I did not measure.
* **I could not explain the 15× per-pass rate swing** (§53), the 5.8× per-second gap between the
  446 s source and the 1800 s sources, or the **superlinear** ride-pass cost (§53c). I report the
  raw pass times and refuse to extrapolate from any of them.
* **I did not obtain a full-chain wall time for the 2.5-hour source.** I ran it twice; neither
  attempt completed even iteration-1 pass A (attempt 1 terminated at 1561.8 s, attempt 2 at
  3711 s at 52 % of the file). §53b answers the *fit* question with a hard lower bound
  (one of seven passes ≥3711 s vs a 1687.5 s budget) and §53c records the anomaly. This is the
  one place where I am reporting a bound where the order asked for a number.
* **I did not isolate the superlinear cost**, and I am flagging it as a risk to the approach
  rather than burying it: the same 9000 s decode took 88.13 s with ebur128 alone and ≥3711 s
  with the ride chain, ≥42× slower.
* **I could not verify the panel's breadth** — §52 shows two of the six are the same audio as a
  third. That is a fact about the panel, not a defect in the measurements.

## 57. Doubts

* **EF4's pass is 0.013 LU wide.** If the panel is ever re-cut (different `inpoint`), EF4 will
  likely flip. I would not treat "5 of 6" as stable.
* **+16 dB and the gap problem are coupled, and the order's item 2 asked me to flag exactly
  this.** Raising `G_max` to clear 4D36's clamp would *increase* the quiet-gap lift on 4D36,
  which is already +13.27 LU and already `ends_above_minus35: true`. I do not think "raise the
  ceiling" is a viable route on its own, and I am saying so rather than leaving it implied.
* **The silence hold has never fired in a real run** (`silent=0` on all six, §49). It is
  implemented, unit-checked in `smoke4.py`, and dead in production on this panel. Any claim
  that the hold protects quiet passages is unproven.
* **The sum moves at 2× the specified slew rate** (§49.2) and that is the order's recipe as
  written, not an implementation error. Whether 1 dB/s is acceptable for a civic broadcast is a
  policy question I have not answered — it is now **Q10** in `questions\U25.md` §13.
* **The whole-program error is one-sided** on all six (−0.10…−0.70, never positive). Something
  in the trim/limiter interaction systematically under-shoots. I have not diagnosed it.
* **I may have killed the first BIG attempt too early.** The clean second attempt went on to
  exceed 3711 s, so terminating at 1561.8 s was a judgement call I would not repeat. I am
  recording it because a killed pass reported as an `ERR` in a log is exactly the kind of thing
  that reads as a filter failure three sessions later.
* **The 30-minute "does not fit" answer is not robust, and I want that said plainly.** All five
  1800 s measurements come from a contended box, so 1188–1877 s are *upper* bounds. At EF4's
  clean per-second rate (0.140 s per source-second) an 1800 s source would take **~252 s** —
  which *would fit* the 300 s on-demand budget, narrowly. So the 30-minute answer is
  **"does not fit at the rates I measured; may fit at the rate the shortest source achieved"**,
  and settling it needs the same source measured on an idle box. The **2.5-hour answer is not
  affected**: 3711 s for one pass out of seven, against 1687.5 s, is not a contention artefact
  of that size.
* **The EDU sentinel.** `quietest_gap` is an arithmetic dB mean over M blocks; ffmpeg's ebur128
  emits `-1000000000.0` for digital-silence blocks, so EDU's raw gap figures read
  `-120000045.235`. `cell4.py` guards the emitted lift to `None` for EDU but the raw source
  value still prints the sentinel. **The sentinel must never be read as a dB figure** — and it
  means the quiet-gap check's *method* is unsound for a source containing digital silence,
  independent of this unit's verdict.

## 58. Bottom line for the coordinator

**STOP, per the order's "if it does not pass" branch. The six-source table and the
curve/chain split after the loop are §46 and §48.**

**The closed loop works and is worth keeping.** It repays 0.145–0.399 LU on every source and it
takes five of six sources inside a two-sided ±0.8 window gate and a ±0.5 whole-program gate
with the limiter and the AAC encoder in the chain. That is a materially better result than
round 3's W45G12.

**It does not pass, for two independent reasons, and only one of them is 4D36:**

1. **4D36 fails both gates** (`wErrLow −1.040`, whole −0.70) because the **+16 dB ceiling binds
   on its iteration-2 curve** — `c1` clamped on 54 of 1801 points, unclamped sum 17.296 — and
   the loop cannot repay what the clamp removes. The predicted iteration-3 clamp is **97**
   points: tightening, not converging. **There is no evidence in this round that a 2-iteration
   closed loop with a finite ceiling converges on a source with 4D36's internal spread.**
2. **Every source lifts the room tone in its quietest gap** by +3.1 to +29.2 LU, and all six
   are flagged `gap_ends_above_minus35: true` (4D36 −21.3, 56F1/6A58 −20.4, E43F emitted
   −21.6). The silence hold never fired once. This is a **product question about how much
   room-tone lift is acceptable between speakers**, and it is now the binding constraint on any
   route that raises `G_max`.

**And a caveat on the panel itself:** EF4, 56F1 and 6A58 are the same recording (§52), so
"5 of 6 pass" is really **3 of the 4 independent recordings** (PARKS, CITY9, NSF) passing;
SUSTAIN — 4D36 — is the only distinct recording that fails. And EF4's pass is 0.013 LU wide.

**Timing (item 5) is a third reason to stop, independent of the loudness verdict.** The
2.5-hour path does not fit either timeout: **one of the seven production passes alone ran
≥3711 s (52 % of the file consumed) against a 1687.5 s warm budget and a 300 s on-demand
budget.** And the ride mechanism itself looks superlinear — the same 9000 s decode took 88.13 s
with ebur128 alone and ≥3711 s with `asetnsamples,asendcmd,volume` in front of it (§53c). If
that survives investigation it is a problem with the command-file approach on long assets, not
with the gain curve.

I have not implemented, not bumped the version, not staged, not committed, and touched no hunk
of `preparer.py` or `stream/_ffmpeg.py`. Worktree clean at `8e9c2ed7`.

---

# Part 5 — Round 5: the Python ride is fast; the loop still misses; STOP

Round-5 order: (1) apply the ride in Python (chunked float32 PCM through an ffmpeg pipe, per-sample
interpolated numpy gain, trim, `alimiter level=0`, AAC, mux) and measure that it runs at decode
speed; (2) iterate to convergence, max 4 iterations, stop when no 240 s window moves > 0.1 LU;
(3) `G_max = +18 dB` with a gap cap; (4) selection gate = worst window (both families) within
−16 ± 0.9 on all six cells and whole program within ±0.5, emitted TP reported, per recording and per
cell; (5) the two timing gates.

**Verdict: STOP branch, per the order's "if it does not pass".** Decision 1 is **met** and is a real
result (§66, §67). Decision 3's gap cap **did not fire where the room-tone lift happens** (§64).
The loop **converges on all seven cells while two of them sit 0.6–0.7 LU below target** (§62): 4D36
and BIG fail the whole-program ±0.5 gate, and 4D36 is **0.001 dB inside** the ±0.9 window gate.
Per recording: SUSTAIN fails in both cuts; PARKS, CITY9 and NSF pass.

## 61. Both failures are an absolute placement offset, not a window-shaping defect

Every emitted 240 s window, mean / sd / extreme spread across both families:

| cell | window mean | sd | min | max | spread |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | −16.479 | 0.085 | −16.550 | −16.335 | 0.215 |
| 56F1 | −16.191 | 0.054 | −16.283 | −16.092 | 0.191 |
| **4D36** | **−16.680** | 0.090 | **−16.899** | **−16.545** | 0.354 |
| EDU | −16.029 | 0.036 | −16.113 | −15.964 | 0.149 |
| 6A58 | −16.169 | 0.067 | −16.338 | −16.034 | 0.304 |
| E43F | −16.100 | 0.053 | −16.180 | −15.989 | 0.191 |
| **BIG** | **−16.644** | 0.060 | **−16.808** | **−16.512** | 0.296 |

**OBSERVED:** on 4D36 not one of its 15 windows is above −16.545; the entire window population sits
0.55–0.90 below target and the whole-program error (−0.700) equals the window-mean error (−0.680).
BIG is the same shape: 75 windows, spread 0.296, all low, whole-program −0.600 vs window-mean
−0.644. **INFERRED:** the ride is *shaping* these cells correctly — no window runs away from the
others — and the whole-program gate fails on a uniform **absolute offset**. That points the
diagnosis at level accounting, not at the curve.

Note also the correlation with source level: the two failing cells are the two quietest sources on
the panel (4D36 source I −27.3, BIG −26.8) and the two highest curve means (12.730, 12.725 dB).

## 62. The residual split (the order's required deliverable)

All seven cells report `converged = True`; all seven emit an artifact that misses −16.0 **low**. This
is where the 0.6–0.7 LU goes.

### 62.1 The loop's Newton step assumes a limiter slope of 1.00; the measured slope is 0.538–0.615 on the failing cells

`trim0 = TARGET − I_A`, where `I_A` is measured on pass A — the ride with **no limiter and no trim**.
Pass B applies `trim0` through the limiter and the correction is `trim1 = trim0 + (TARGET − I_B)`,
which assumes 1 dB of trim buys 1 dB of emitted loudness. The limiter's real slope is
`(I_C − I_B) / (trim1 − trim0)`, and `cell5.py` measures both ends of it every iteration.

Measured slope, per cell and iteration:

| cell | it1 | it2 | it3 | it4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 0.700 | 0.727 | 0.727 | — |
| 56F1 | 0.800 | 0.833 | 0.833 | 0.833 |
| **4D36** | **0.571** | **0.571** | **0.600** | — |
| EDU | 0.667 | 1.000 | 1.000 | — |
| 6A58 | 0.800 | 0.833 | 0.833 | 0.833 |
| E43F | 0.800 | 0.800 | 0.833 | — |
| **BIG** | **0.615** | **0.538** | **0.538** | **0.571** |

The two SUSTAIN cells sit at 0.538–0.615; the five that pass sit at 0.667–1.000. So the step repays
only ~0.6 of the shortfall on the cells that need it most, and the arithmetic closes:

```
residual = (I_B − TARGET) × (1 − slope)
4D36 it3:  (−17.5 − (−16.0)) × (1 − 0.600) = 1.5 × 0.400 = 0.600
BIG  it4:  (−17.4 − (−16.0)) × (1 − 0.571) = 1.4 × 0.429 = 0.600
```

**The formula predicts the measured whole-program residual on both failing cells to within 0.1 LU**
(measured −0.700 and −0.600). **INFERRED:** the loop's failure on SUSTAIN is the limiter's gain
compression, and the loop contains no term that models it. This is a defect in the loop, not in the
curve.

### 62.2 `trim0` is recomputed from scratch every iteration against an unlimited pass whose level is climbing

Per-iteration trajectory (`cells.json` → `iterations[]`), the failing two and one passing cell for
contrast:

```
4D36  it1  I_A=-16.0  trim0=+0.0  I_B=-17.4  trim1=+1.4  I_C=-16.6
      it2  I_A=-15.4  trim0=-0.6  I_B=-17.4  trim1=+0.8  I_C=-16.6
      it3  I_A=-14.7  trim0=-1.3  I_B=-17.5  trim1=+0.2  I_C=-16.6   move=0.090 -> converged
BIG   it1  I_A=-16.0  trim0=+0.0  I_B=-17.3  trim1=+1.3  I_C=-16.5
      it2  I_A=-15.4  trim0=-0.6  I_B=-17.3  trim1=+0.7  I_C=-16.6
      it3  I_A=-14.8  trim0=-1.2  I_B=-17.3  trim1=+0.1  I_C=-16.6
      it4  I_A=-14.1  trim0=-1.9  I_B=-17.4  trim1=-0.5  I_C=-16.6   move=0.057 -> converged
EDU   it1  I_A=-16.0  trim0=+0.0  I_B=-16.3  trim1=+0.3  I_C=-16.1
      it2  I_A=-15.9  trim0=-0.1  I_B=-16.4  trim1=+0.3  I_C=-16.0
      it3  I_A=-15.9  trim0=-0.1  I_B=-16.4  trim1=+0.3  I_C=-16.0   move=0.092 -> converged
```

`I_A` — the loudness of the ridden material *before* any limiter and any trim — **rises 0.5–0.7 dB
per iteration** on 4D36 and BIG as the curve is corrected, so `trim0 = TARGET − I_A` walks down
(+0.0 → −1.9 dB on BIG) without ever accumulating a correction. The curve is being re-aimed at
`TARGET − I_A` where `I_A` is a level the limiter never lets out. **INFERRED:** curve and trim are
correcting against each other; neither converges on the emitted level. EDU, whose slope is 1.000,
shows the same walk with no consequence — which is the control.

### 62.3 `I_C` is invariant to the trim, so the stop rule is satisfied at a fixed point that is not the target

Range of `I_C` across a cell's iterations vs how far `trim0` moved:

| cell | I_C values | I_C range | trim0 walk | final move | whole-program err |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | −16.3, −16.3, −16.3 | **0.0** | 1.0 dB | 0.055 | −0.400 |
| 56F1 | −16.1 ×4 | **0.0** | 0.6 dB | 0.057 | −0.200 |
| **4D36** | −16.6 ×3 | **0.0** | 1.3 dB | 0.090 | **−0.700** |
| EDU | −16.1, −16.0, −16.0 | 0.1 | 0.1 dB | 0.092 | 0.000 |
| 6A58 | −16.1 ×4 | **0.0** | 0.6 dB | 0.060 | −0.200 |
| E43F | −16.1 ×3 | **0.0** | 0.3 dB | 0.055 | −0.100 |
| **BIG** | −16.5, −16.6 ×3 | 0.1 | 1.9 dB | 0.057 | **−0.600** |

**OBSERVED:** moving `trim0` by up to 1.9 dB moves the emitted loudness `I_C` by at most 0.1 LU, on
every cell. The order's stop rule ("no 240 s window moves by more than 0.1 LU") is therefore
satisfied at a point that is *pinned*, not *converged*: on 4D36 and BIG the pin sits 0.6 LU below
target and the loop cannot see the difference. **`converged = True` on all seven cells is not
evidence that the target was reached.**

**Residual split, as the order asks.** Of the 0.6–0.7 LU whole-program miss on the two failing cells:

* **≈0.60 LU — the un-modelled limiter slope** (§62.1; predicted 0.600 on both, measured −0.700 and
  −0.600).
* **≈0.10 LU — the emit/re-measure delta** between pass C and the emitted artifact. Measured on all
  seven cells (`emitted_I − I_C`): −0.10, −0.10, −0.10, 0.00, −0.10, 0.00, 0.00.
* **No third term.** The curve's window shape is within 0.354 LU spread on the worst cell (§61), and
  the window-mean error accounts for the whole-program error.

## 63. The +18 dB ceiling binds only on the two failing cells, and does not itself make a window outlier

| cell | curve min | curve max | curve mean | hits `g_max` | base-curve hits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | −5.242 | 15.253 | 7.244 | 0 / 447 | 0 |
| 56F1 | −1.665 | 16.430 | 5.653 | 0 / 1801 | 0 |
| **4D36** | **+8.000** | **+18.000** | **12.730** | **21 / 1801** | **0** |
| EDU | 6.725 | 15.517 | 10.102 | 0 / 1801 | 0 |
| 6A58 | −1.644 | 16.369 | 5.471 | 0 / 1801 | 0 |
| E43F | 6.700 | 14.420 | 10.549 | 0 / 1801 | 0 |
| **BIG** | 4.386 | **+18.000** | **12.725** | **101 / 9001** | **0** |

(`final_curve` diagnostics; `base_curve` is the pre-loop curve for the same cell.)

**OBSERVED:** the +18 dB ceiling is hit **only** on 4D36 (21 points) and BIG (101 points), and on
both cells the *base* curve hit it 0 times. The loop drove them into the ceiling while chasing the
0.6 LU it never recovered. 4D36's emitted curve lives entirely between the gap-cap floor and the
ceiling: min exactly +8.000, max exactly +18.000.

**OBSERVED:** the clamp did not by itself produce an outlying window — 4D36's window spread is 0.354
and every window is *low*, so the clamp region is not making one window misbehave. **INFERRED:** the
ceiling is a symptom, not the cause, of this round's failure. Raising it from +16 (round 4) to +18
cleared round 4's specific clamp failure without clearing the target miss.

## 64. Decision 3's gap cap did not fire where the room-tone lift happens

Order item 3 asks for the gap cap *and* for the quiet-gap check re-run. Both were run. The cap is
inert on exactly the cells that lift, and five of seven cells end above −35 LUFS.

Quiet-gap check — source vs emitted short-term, in each source's own quietest 10 s:

| cell | src quietest 10 s @ | source mean (LUFS) | emitted mean (LUFS) | gain applied | ends above −35 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 406.9 s | −35.187 | −31.637 | +3.550 dB | **yes** |
| 56F1 | 1164.3 s | −33.849 | −19.841 | **+14.008 dB** | **yes** |
| **4D36** | 851.5 s | −34.568 | −21.451 | **+13.117 dB** | **yes** |
| EDU | 784.6 s | *sentinel* (−120000045.235) | −52.554 | n/a (sentinel) | no |
| 6A58 | 1239.4 s | −33.849 | −19.823 | **+14.026 dB** | **yes** |
| E43F | 147.0 s | −50.776 | −37.710 | +13.066 dB | no |
| **BIG** | 5200.9 s | −36.040 | −20.598 | **+15.442 dB** | **yes** |

Gap-cap diagnostics on the curve actually emitted, last iteration with a `cap_after`:

| cell | gain in quietest 10 s | `n_gap_points` | of which abs / rel | `n_gap_points_capped` | `cap_depth_max_db` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | +3.550 | 7 | 1 / 6 | **0** | 0.000 |
| 56F1 | +14.008 | 3 | 1 / 2 | **0** | 0.000 |
| 4D36 | +13.117 | 1 | 1 / 0 | 1 | 0.500 |
| EDU | n/a | 49 | 34 / 15 | 40 | 3.967 |
| 6A58 | +14.026 | 2 | 1 / 1 | **0** | 0.000 |
| E43F | +13.066 | 158 | 39 / 119 | 143 | 5.200 |
| BIG | +15.442 | 1 | 1 / 0 | 1 | 0.690 |

**OBSERVED:** the cap engages heavily on the two cells that do **not** flag — EDU (40 of 49 points
capped, depth 3.967 dB) and E43F (143 of 158, depth 5.200 dB) — and caps **0 of 3** gap points on
56F1 and **0 of 2** on 6A58, the two cells being ridden up +14 dB. On 4D36 it finds **1** gap point
in 1800 s and on BIG **1** in 9000 s, each capped by ≤0.69 dB, while the quietest 10 s on those same
cells is lifted +13.117 / +15.442 dB.

**INFERRED (mechanism, from the rule's two criteria as coded).** A block counts as a gap when the
emitted momentary loudness is below the **−70 LUFS absolute** gate *or* more than **20 LU below its
window's gated loudness**. On a quiet recording neither branch can fire: 56F1's source quietest 10 s
measures −33.849 LUFS against a window gated loudness of roughly −28 to −30, i.e. only 4–7 LU below
that window's own program, and ~35 LU above the −70 gate. The rule is written in absolute terms, and
room tone in a quiet recording is loud in absolute terms. EDU and E43F contain genuine near-silence
and are capped correctly. **This is a product question about what "between speakers" should mean on
a quiet source, and I have not changed the rule.** It is now **Q11** in `questions\U25.md`.

## 65. The emitted +3.9 / +4.4 dBFS true peak is one non-reproducible frame

Order item 4 asks that emitted TP be reported. Both failing cells carry a positive true peak far
above the −1.0 dBFS ceiling. I chased it rather than reporting the number and moving on.

Measured chain on 4D36 (`glitch7`–`glitch10`):

```
source, loudest 1024-sample frame                     -3.692 dBFS
ridden PCM, pre-limiter                                +13.162 sample / +13.2 TP
ridden PCM, post-limiter (captured from the ride)      -2.999997 sample / -1.1 TP
the same PCM -> aac 192k -> mpegts (emit's own flags)  -1.806124 sample / -1.0 TP
re-emit of the shipped curve+trim, byte-clean          -1.806124 sample / -1.0 TP
the SHIPPED artifact                                   +3.378333 sample / +3.9 TP
```

`alimiter=limit=0.707946:...:asc=0` hard-clips at `limit`, so a signal that passed through it cannot
carry a sample above 0.707946 (−2.999997 dBFS). The shipped artifact carries 1.47542. I re-ran the
emit path exactly as `cell5.py` runs it, same curve, same trim, same sink args (`glitch9` E1) and
measured both files as signals (`glitch10`):

| | bytes | md5 | sample peak | TP | I | LRA |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shipped `4D36/emitted.r5.ts` | 54,320,720 | `351734b35bd3c309df8a9d9cabf4fe2f` | +3.378 dBFS | +3.9 | −16.7 | 5.0 |
| re-emit `4D36_reemit.ts` | 54,327,676 | `2d777accbddc58cd0de335d91bcee1b5` | −1.806 dBFS | −1.0 | **−16.7** | **5.0** |

Whole-file per-1024-frame ratio ship/re-emit over 84,377 frames: **median +0.000 dB** (p01 −0.219,
p99 +0.226). Census: shipped has exactly **1** frame above 0 dBFS (t = 586.411 s, ch1, peak
1.47542, +3.378 dBFS), second-loudest −1.833 dBFS; the re-emit has **0**.

**OBSERVED:** the shipped 4D36 artifact is the same signal as its own re-emit except for a **single
corrupted frame**, `I` and `LRA` identical to 0.1 LU / 0.1 LU, and the defect **does not reproduce**.
**BIG's +4.4 was not re-emitted** (artifact `BIG/emitted.r5.ts`, 271,630,108 B, md5
`55042491c52f4d55f2d0adb3aead4417`, I −16.6) — I report it as *measured on the shipped artifact, not
reproduced*, with the 4D36 case as the same-class prior. This is a real defect worth chasing
(**Q13**), but it is not why the loop misses its target: fixing it would move the emitted TP, not
the 0.6 LU.

Started 09:49:14, exited 10:02 with `exit=0`. The harness sets its own class first
(`self priority class: BELOW_NORMAL`, `out/timing5.log:1`) and asserts the builder's audio flags
rather than hard-coding them (`out/timing5.log:2`). Raw result lines:

```
[BIG] video=503.4s ride=114.2s mux=9.8s total=627.5s (0.0697 s/s) budget=1688s -> PASS headroom=2.69x  prio(video)=BELOW_NORMAL
[ONDEMAND] video=77.5s ride=23.3s mux=1.0s total=101.7s (0.0565 s/s) budget=300s -> PASS headroom=2.95x  prio(video)=BELOW_NORMAL
```

Full records (`out/timing.json`):

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BIG` warm-2.5 h | 9000.0 | 0.0 | 1 | 503.44 | 114.22 | 9.85 | **627.51** | 1687.5 | 1059.99 | **2.689×** | **PASS** |
| `ONDEMAND` 30 min | 1800.022 | 2809.997 | 8 | 77.48 | 23.26 | 0.99 | **101.73** | 300.0 | 198.27 | **2.949×** | **PASS** |

Supporting fields, both cases: `child_priority_class {video: BELOW_NORMAL, mux: BELOW_NORMAL}`,
`ride_sink_rc 0`, `ride_dec_rc 0`, `ride_peak_mb 108.7`, `muxed_duration_s` 9000.021 / 1800.033.
BIG: 431,999,952 ride frames, 8999.999 audio-seconds, video 3,440,063,856 B, audio 271,619,204 B,
muxed 3,676,584,212 B. ONDEMAND: 86,400,960 frames, 1800.02 s, muxed 702,867,328 B.

**OBSERVED, and worth stating plainly: the loudness chain is not what makes this slow.** Video is
80.2 % of the warm 2.5 h chain (503.4 of 627.5 s) and 76.2 % of the on-demand path (77.5 of
101.7 s). Everything U25 adds — ride and mux — is **19.8 %** of the warm chain (124.07 s, of which
the ride stage is 114.22 s) and **23.8 %** of the on-demand path (24.25 s). Even if U25's audio
work were free, the 2.5 h warm encode would still take 513 s.

**Note on the budget figure.** The order writes 1691 s; the harness computed `max(300, 9000/8*1.5)`
= **1687.5 s** and recorded that. Both gates pass by more than 1000 s / 198 s respectively, so the
4 s difference does not matter.

**INFERRED (decomposition of the ride stage).** The timing harness's ride stage writes `audio.ts`
through `R.encode_sink_args`, so its 114.22 s **includes the AAC encode**, unlike the ride-only pass
times in §67. `cells.json` records BIG's standalone encode at 118.22 s and its ride-only passes at
33.7–41.2 s, which is consistent with 114.22 s = ride + encode.

**Load caveat (OBSERVED).** These are not idle-machine numbers. Throughout, the box ran the live
3-channel station (three long-lived ffmpeg encoders under a supervised python started 07:26:54 —
listed in §69, untouched by me). A third party's python
(`…\civiccast-ds-u30\.venv\Scripts\python.exe repro7.py …`) started at **09:59:19**, i.e. during the
ONDEMAND case, and was still running at 10:03. The gates pass anyway, which is the stronger result;
an idle re-run would only move the margin.

## 67. Q9 is answered by measurement

Round 4's open risk — that the `asendcmd` ride made a 2.5 h asset unaffordable — is closed by your
decision 1 and confirmed here. All figures OBSERVED, same box, same 9000 s source:

| 9000 s pass | wall | × realtime | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `anull,ebur128=peak=true` only (§53c baseline) | 88.13 s | 102× | `BIG.series.json:wall_s` |
| `asetnsamples=n=256,asendcmd,volume` + ebur128 | ≥3711 s at 52 % of the file | ≤2.4× | §53a |
| **Python chunked ride, one full pass (it1 A/B/C)** | **33.67 / 35.60 / 34.70 s** | **≈255×** | `cells.json` BIG |

Per-second-of-audio cost is **flat** across a 20× length range — EF4 0.0040 s/s (446 s),
4D36 0.0033 s/s (1800 s), BIG 0.0037–0.0046 s/s (9000 s) — so §53c's superlinearity was a property
of `asendcmd`, not of the box or the source. Memory is bounded: 327 chunks of 524,288 B, peak
working set 106,778,624 B (`out/smoke.json`); the timing harness independently records
`ride_peak_mb 108.7` on both a 30-minute and a 2.5-hour asset.

**Answer: the `asendcmd` mechanism is retired and nothing further is needed from you on Q9.**

## 68. Defects in my own round-5 harness

Reported because they are in the artifacts the coordinator may read:

1. **The quiet-gap sentinel.** `cell5.py`'s `gain_applied_db` does not guard ebur128's
   digital-silence sentinel. For EDU the source's quietest-10 s momentary is
   **−120000045.235 LUFS**, so the computed gain is `119999992.681 dB` — non-physical. The
   `flagged_above_-35` outcome (False) and the emitted mean (−52.554) are both correct; **do not
   read EDU's `gain_applied_db` as a measurement.** `cells.json` carries the raw value.
2. **4D36's window gate margin is 0.001 dB.** `emitted_worst_window = −16.899` against a bound of
   −16.9. It passes `cell5.py`'s 0.9 test and fails `validate.TOL = 0.8`, and I report it as a
   window-gate pass *only* in the arithmetic sense — the cell fails on the whole program regardless.
3. **The 5-lens/one-line summary of my own earlier digest used the wrong slope formula**
   (`(I_C − I_A)/trim0`); §62.1 uses the correct `(I_C − I_B)/(trim1 − trim0)` and reproduces the
   0.538–1.000 range. If a number from that earlier dump disagrees with this Part 5, Part 5 is the
   corrected one.
4. **I read the live station's upload files with ffmpeg.** No mp4 copy of any of the four sources
   exists anywhere under `%TEMP%\u25` (checked), so every measurement in this round decoded from
   `C:\ProgramData\CivicCast\data\uploads\…` in place. All of it was read-only (`-i <path>`, output
   to `%TEMP%`), nothing was written under `C:\ProgramData`, no **station** service, process or
   schedule was started, stopped or touched, and no file was locked or modified. (The ffmpeg
   processes I did start are my own scratch children, all exited — §69.) I would rather flag it than let
   "read the live station only" be read as "never opened its files".

## 70. What I did NOT do, and what I could not verify

**Not done** (the order's STOP branch, plus the standing constraints):

* No implementation of any kind: no chain code in `preparer.py` or `stream/_ffmpeg.py`, no constants,
  no pure functions for curve / gap cap / slew / gating / loop / chunked PCM ride, no tests.
* `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` **not** bumped.
* Not staged, not committed, not pushed, not tagged, not merged. U25 remains unmerged with U29; the
  U29 hunks in the same file are untouched.
* Nothing written to the worktree at all; no scratch files in it.
* The gap rule is unchanged (Q11), the trim step is unchanged (Q12), the TP frame was not chased to
  an encoder-level cause (Q13), and decision 1's fallback path (measurement failure → old single-pass
  + one WARNING, not cached under the new key) is **not implemented** — it only becomes code if the
  round is implemented.

**Could not verify:**

* **BIG's emitted true peak by re-emission.** I measured +4.4 dBFS on the shipped artifact but did
  not re-emit BIG (the cell is 2.5 h and the re-emit would have to be checked frame-by-frame); I
  cannot say whether it is the same single-frame defect as 4D36's or something else.
* **The cause of the corrupt frame.** I know what it is not (the limiter, the codec on a clean input,
  the ride's pipe, the curve, the trim) and that it does not reproduce, but not whether it is the
  AAC/mpegts writer, the hardware video encoder path, or a disk/IO event under load.
* **Whether the loop's slope error is the whole story.** The `residual = (I_B − T) × (1 − slope)`
  formula fits both failing cells to 0.1 LU from two points each (pass B and pass C), which is a
  thin fit. See §71.
* **The station's own live loudnorm configuration** was not re-read this round; every number here is
  from the scratch harness in `%TEMP%\u25`, not from the running station's pipeline.
* **Whether `max_iters` 4 is enough.** The order caps at 4; 4D36 converged at 3 and BIG at 4 with the
  stop rule satisfied. I did not run a 5th or 6th iteration to see whether the pin is truly a fixed
  point or merely slow.

## 71. Doubts

1. **The slope explanation could be a 2-point coincidence.** Per cell I have one measured slope per
   iteration and one `I_B`/`I_C` pair. It fits both failing cells to 0.1 LU and explains why the
   five passing cells pass, which is more than a coincidence would usually manage — but the
   disconfirming test is cheap and I did not run it: **run 4D36 with `max_iters = 6` and watch
   `I_C`.** If it walks to −16.0 within 0.1 LU, my "pinned fixed point" claim is wrong and the loop
   is merely slow; if it stays at −16.6 while `trim0` keeps falling, the pin is real. That single
   run decides between "fix the trim accounting" (Q12) and "give the loop more iterations".
2. **§64's mechanism is inferred, not traced.** I argue the gap rule's two criteria cannot fire on a
   quiet source from the source and emitted levels (−33.849 LUFS against a window gated loudness of
   roughly −28…−30 and a −70 gate). I did not instrument the predicate to log, per block, which
   branch was evaluated and what it returned. If the cap is inert for a different reason — e.g. the
   blocks are evaluated at a grid offset that misses the quietest 10 s — then changing the
   thresholds (Q11) would not fix it.
3. **4D36's window gate sits on a knife edge.** −16.899 against −16.9. A re-measure could land either
   side. I report it as a pass in that one predicate and a fail overall, but no decision should rest
   on that 0.001 dB.
4. **The numbers were taken on a loaded box.** The station was live throughout and a second agent
   started work mid-run. If the coordinator wants a floor rather than a headline, the timing gates
   should be re-run when the box is quieter — I expect better, not worse, but I have not measured it.
5. **Nothing here tells the coordinator what the station *should* do** about room tone (Q11). The
   round-5 data shows the cap does not fire; it does not say whether a +14 dB lift of a −33.8 LUFS
   room tone is actually wrong for a civic broadcast. That is a product judgement and I am not
   making it.

## 72. Bottom line for the coordinator

**STOP branch, as instructed.** In the order's own sequence:

1. **Decision 1 — MET.** The chunked Python ride runs at 33.7–41.2 s per 9000 s pass (≈255× realtime),
   flat per-second cost over a 20× length range, bounded at 108.7 MB. Q9 is closed.
2. **Decision 5 — MET.** Both timing gates pass on a loaded box: 627.5 s of 1687.5 s for the 2.5 h
   warm chain (2.689× headroom), 101.7 s of 300 s for the 30-minute on-demand path (2.949×). Video
   dominates both; U25 adds 19.8 % / 23.8 %.
3. **Decision 4 — NOT MET.** 4D36 fails whole program at **−0.700** and BIG at **−0.600** against
   ±0.5; 4D36 also sits on the window gate's edge (0.899 of 0.9, and fails the stricter 0.8).
   Per recording: PARKS pass, CITY9 pass, NSF pass, **SUSTAIN fails in both cuts**.
4. **Residual split.** ≈**0.60 LU** un-modelled limiter compression (`residual = (I_B − TARGET) ×
   (1 − slope)`, slope measured 0.538–0.615 on the two failing cells and 0.667–1.000 on the five
   that pass), plus ≈**0.10 LU** emit/re-measure delta. No third term: the curve's window shape is
   flat to 0.354 LU on the worst cell, and the whole-program error equals the window-mean error.
   The loop reports `converged = True` on all seven cells because the stop rule measures window
   *movement* of a quantity the trim barely moves (`I_C` varies ≤0.1 LU while `trim0` walks 1.9 dB).
5. **Decision 3 — the gap cap did not fire where the lift happens.** 0 of 3 gap points capped on
   56F1 and 0 of 2 on 6A58 while both were ridden up ~+14 dB; 1 point each on 4D36 and BIG, capped by
   ≤0.69 dB. **Five of seven cells end above −35 LUFS** in their quietest 10 s; the two heaviest
   lifts are +13.117 dB (4D36) and +15.442 dB (BIG). The cap engages heavily only on the two cells
   that do *not* flag (EDU 40/49, E43F 143/158). → **Q11**, a product question, unanswered by me.
6. **One more defect, separate from the level question.** The emitted true peaks on the two failing
   cells (+3.9 / +4.4 dBFS) are a **single corrupted AAC frame** on 4D36 that **does not reproduce**
   — the shipped file and a byte-clean re-emit are identical signal except that one frame, with
   identical I (−16.7) and LRA (5.0). BIG's is measured but not reproduced. → **Q13**.

**What I would do with the coordinator's answer, in priority order:** (1) answer Q12 — the slope fix
is the only item on this list that changes the measured level result, and §71.1 gives a one-run test
that confirms or kills it first; (2) answer Q11 — the cap is inert, so as specified it does not do
the job the order gave it; (3) decide Q10, still open from round 4 (the summed curve moves at up to
2× the specified slew); (4) file Q13 as its own beta.10 defect.

```text
proved: `python cell5.py --only EF4,56F1,4D36,EDU,6A58,E43F,BIG` -> 7/7 cells converged, 5/7 pass,
        4D36 whole-program -0.700 and BIG -0.600 vs +/-0.5 (STOP);
        `python timing5.py --only ONDEMAND,BIG` -> BIG 627.51s/1687.5s PASS 2.689x,
        ONDEMAND 101.73s/300s PASS 2.949x; both BELOW_NORMAL, ride rc 0/0
  lane: Standard (diagnostic round; no product code written, worktree clean at 8e9c2ed7)
```

## 76. A harness-integrity finding: round 5's BIG "PASS" ignored the true-peak gate

**OBSERVED.** The round-5 BIG record in `%TEMP%\u25\r5\out\cells_q12.json` already contained all
three of these fields:

```text
"emitted_TP_dBFS": 3.3,  "tp_under_ceiling": false,  ...  "passes": true
```

`TP_CEILING = -1.0` is defined at `%TEMP%\u25\r5\cell5b.py:57`. The harness therefore **measured**
BIG's emitted true peak at +3.3 dBTP, **computed** that it breaches the −1.0 dBTP ceiling, stored
both facts, and then decided `passes` without them:

```python
# %TEMP%\u25\r5\cell5b.py:379
rep["passes"] = bool(rep["gates"]["worst_window_within_0.9"]
                     and rep["gates"]["whole_program_within_0.5"])
```

`tp_under_ceiling` never enters the expression. Consequences, both OBSERVED:

- **The round-5 acceptance table in §60 overstates BIG.** BIG's PASS was a window-and-level PASS; it
  was never a true-peak-compliant PASS. This corrects my own prior report, not the coordinator's.
- **4D36 passes the TP gate only by equality** — `emitted_TP_dBFS = -1.0` against a `-1.0` ceiling.
  It is `<=`, not `<`, and the value is printed to one decimal.

This is my harness, in temp, outside the repo, and it is already disclosed in §68 as a suspected
class of error. It is nonetheless a real defect in the evidence the round-5 acceptance rests on, and
any acceptance decision made from that table has to be recomputed with the gate switched on. The
recomputation is itself an option put to the coordinator (§22 option (c)).

## 77. Cause — the limiter holds; the ride simply has no true-peak ceiling

The competing explanations were "the limiter leaks" (ride-side, fixable in the ride) and "the
overshoot is added after the limiter" (encoder-side). The probe `%TEMP%\u25\q13_cause.py` separates
them.

**OBSERVED — meter semantics first, because nothing else can be read until the two peaks are
distinguished.** A full-scale 11 kHz tone through the ride's exact limiter chain:

```bash
ffmpeg -f f32le -ar 48000 -ac 1 -i hot_11kHz.f32 \
  -af "alimiter=limit=0.707946:attack=5:release=50:asc=0:level=0,ebur128=peak=true" -f null -
```

```text
  FTPK:  -3.0 dBFS  TPK:  -2.6 dBFS
  True peak:
    Peak:       -2.6 dBFS
```

- `FTPK` — the sample peak **after** the limiter — reads **−3.0 dBFS, which is the limiter's ceiling
  exactly** (`limit=0.707946` = 10^(−3/20)). The limiter is not leaking.
- ebur128's end-of-run `Peak:` is the **true** peak (`TPK`), 0.4 dB above the sample peak on this
  worst-case tone. That is the meter the harness's `TP_C` and `emitted_TP_seekfree` come from, so
  those two numbers are true-peak readings and must not be compared to a sample-peak ceiling.

**OBSERVED — the two ends of BIG's own chain, same program, same file (`cells_q12.json`):**

| point in the chain | value | where |
|---|---|---|
| source true peak | −0.4 dBTP | BIG `source_TP` |
| post-gain, post-limiter, **pre-AAC** | **−0.8 dBTP** | BIG `iterations[0..2].TP_C` — identical in all three |
| **emitted** | **+3.3 dBTP** | BIG `emitted.emitted_TP_seekfree` |

**INFERRED (labelled — this is not measured):** the ~4.1 dB between the limiter's output and the
emitted artifact arrives in the AAC encode/decode. I did **not** isolate the encoder with a single
controlled PCM-in/AAC-out pass, so "all 4.1 dB is the encoder" is an inference from the two
endpoints, not an isolated measurement. It is consistent with the raw scan, which finds the frames
in the decoded AAC, not in the ride's PCM.

**OBSERVED — the design gap, read from the repo.** The incumbent conform bounds true peak explicitly:

- `civiccast/egress/preparer.py:2415` — `-af loudnorm=I={loudness_target_lufs:g}:LRA=11:TP=-1.5`
  (single-pass branch)
- `civiccast/egress/preparer.py:2422-2428` — the measured branch, ending `":linear=true"`

`loudnorm`'s limiter is true-peak aware. That is the only true-peak protection the shipped path has.
The ride replaces `loudnorm` entirely and puts `alimiter=limit=0.707946` in its place — a **sample
peak** ceiling 6 dB lower in nominal terms, and blind to inter-sample and codec overshoot. BIG's
source arrives already at −0.4 dBTP and then receives roughly +11 dB of ride gain (`trim=1.8886`
plus the curve), so a positive emitted true peak is the expected consequence of the design, not an
anomaly.

**INFERRED (labelled):** the incumbent path would land BIG's emitted true peak near its −1.5 dBTP
target rather than at +3.3. I did **not** re-encode BIG through the incumbent path to measure it. The
coordinator said to stop; that run is ~15 minutes and I did not spend it. Treat "the incumbent is
better here" as unmeasured.

## 78. Q14 — the product question, filed, not answered

**Q14: what should the station do when the ride it is being given has no true-peak ceiling and
deterministically emits samples above full scale on one of the two assets it was validated
against?** Written up in full in `questions/U25.md` §21, with three options and my recommendation
((b) then (c): give the ride a true-peak-aware output stage, then re-validate with the harness gate
actually switched on). This is a product decision — whether a public-record artifact peaking at
+2.754 dBFS may ship — so per the brief's rule 5 I stopped rather than inventing an answer. The
acceptance consequence is that option (b) changes the limiter, which changes `TP_C`, which changes
the trim convergence, so **the whole round-5 acceptance would need re-running** before anything is
built.

**Q10's window effect is deferred on purpose.** `cell5c.py` (generated from `cell5b.py` by
`mk5c.py`, one added `R.slew_limit` call on the summed curve) is written and unrun. Answer 5 asked
for the summed curve's slew to be clamped *in the implementation* and any window effect noted. If
option (b) is taken, the ceiling change invalidates any window number measured now — so running it
while stopped would produce a number the likely answer discards. It is not an input to Q14. To be
disclosed as **NOT DONE**, with that reason, rather than reported as a number.

  `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` bump to `"loudnorm-v3-ride"`; the `_LOUDNESS_METHOD_RIDE` constant; the
  `loudness_method` docstring extension). I reverted them rather than leave a half-wired file whose
  `_cache_key` change would be live in the file U29 is editing. The exact patch is saved at
  `%TEMP%\u25\preparer_hunks.patch` (85 lines) and I proved it round-trips: `git apply --check -R` on
  the applied tree printed `REVERSE_OK`; after `git checkout -- civiccast/egress/preparer.py`,
  `git apply --check` printed `FORWARD_OK`. Re-applying it is one command. **It does not compile as
  saved** — `sliding_levels` is missing from the import list and must be added (alphabetically after
  `run_ride`) when the wiring is redone.
- **`civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py` is untracked and unwired.** ~1000 lines, `__all__` at 109,
  pure functions and arg builders described in `questions/U25.md` §22.

**What I did NOT do:**

- **Did not implement the ride in `preparer.py`** — not the segment path, not the whole-asset conform
  path. Answer 5 authorized it conditionally, the condition on Q13 failed, and the order's own text
  for that outcome is "stop and report".
- **Did not run Q10 / `cell5c.py`** — reason in §78, not an oversight.
- **Did not isolate the AAC encoder** with a controlled single pass, so the ~4.1 dB split in §77 is
  INFERRED, not measured.
- **Did not re-encode BIG through the incumbent `loudnorm` path** to measure its true peak, so the
  claim "the incumbent would do better" is INFERRED, not measured.
- **Did not touch `automation.py`.** Its rollover lead is derived from the per-pass timeout
  (`_ROLLOVER_PREPARATION_PASSES = 2.0`, automation.py:995) while the ride needs many more passes
  per conform than the incumbent's two; that is a real coupling for whoever implements the ride, and
  it belongs to a file outside my list. Surfaced, not changed. `automation.py:982` also carries a
  now-stale comment naming `build_loudnorm_probe_args`.
- **Did not fix the `passes` gate in `cell5b.py`** — it is disclosed in §76 and is put to the
  coordinator as option (c), because recomputing round 5's acceptance is an acceptance decision, not
  a harness cleanup.
- **Did not re-run the egress gate suite this round.** No tracked file changed, so there is no new
  candidate to gate. The baseline from before this round stands: `python -m pytest tests/egress -q
  -p no:randomly` → `2 failed, 1750 passed, 43 skipped in 179.05s`, of which exactly **1** is

  (`test_hls_sink_captions.py::test_packaged_gstreamer_to_hls_sink_preserves_captions_and_cadence`,
  `AssertionError: packaged GStreamer runtime unavailable`, proven in the `git archive 8e9c2ed7`
  export). The other is `test_two_pass_cache_key_includes_method`, broken by my own
  `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` bump and **moot now that `preparer.py` is reverted**.
- **Did not touch the live station.** The three `ffmpeg.exe` processes visible on the box are
  Session-0 (the station's own, PIDs 29112, 18784, 37732); I did not touch them. No `ffmpeg.exe` of
  mine remains. I ran at most one of my own at a time throughout.

**Doubts:**

1. **The biggest one is not technical.** BIG's +2.754 dBFS is real, deterministic and reproducible,
   and the artifact it would be shipping is a public-record meeting recording. I do not think a
   sample-peak-only ceiling is the right contract for this product, but I also cannot rule out that
   the coordinator will prefer to ship the ride and track the ceiling separately — that is exactly
   why it is filed rather than fixed.
2. **The 4.1 dB is not fully attributed.** Two endpoints and a plausible mechanism is not the same
   as isolating the encoder. If the coordinator wants the fix sized (how far below −3 dBFS the
   limiter must sit to hold +? dBTP after AAC), that isolation has to be run first, and it may show
   the overshoot is not constant across content — the four frames sit at +0.957 to +2.754, so it
   plainly is not.
3. **The window numbers in §74 were measured on the current chain.** If option (b) changes the
   ceiling, the trim convergence moves and those windows may not survive. I flag this rather than
   promise them.
4. **The harness defect found in §76 makes me less confident in round 5's table as a whole.** I
   found one gate that was computed and discarded; I did not audit the other gates for the same
   shape of error. That audit is cheap and should happen before any acceptance claim is built on
   that table.

# Part 7 — Answer 6 executed end to end. **STOP with the table.** No product change.

Answer 6 was "Q14 is engineering, not product. (b) then (c). Build it", with six decisions and a stop
rule. I ran all six decisions and the acceptance panel. The panel does **not** pass, so per the
order's own rule I wrote `questions/U25.md` §23–§25 and stopped. **Nothing in `preparer.py`, no new
code, no commits.**

The full write-up for the coordinator is `questions/U25.md` §23–§25. This part is the evidence
record: commands, raw results, and the OBSERVED/INFERRED split.

## 82. The stop determination, applied literally

The order: *"If a cell fails only on TP by <= 0.3 dB after the guard, implement anyway and report
it; any other failure: STOP with the table."*

- 4D36 is the only cell that fails *only* on TP, and it misses by **0.90 dB**, i.e. 3× the 0.3 dB
  tolerance. The implement-anyway branch requires the miss to be ≤ 0.3 dB. It is not.
- EF4 and BIG fail the loudness gates, not TP-only.

→ **STOP with the table.** (OBSERVED; the rule is mechanical, not a judgement call)

## 83. The control that isolates the cause — guard off, all 7 cells

I added this run because the stop report would otherwise say "the panel fails" without saying *what
fails*. Command:

```
python cell5b.py --only EF4,56F1,4D36,EDU,6A58,E43F,BIG --guard-off
```

`--guard-off` sets `max_reemits=0`: the artifact is emitted at the nominal −1.50 dBTP ceiling and
never re-emitted. → `out/run_5b_guardoff_all.log`, JSON `out/cells_q12.guardoff_all.json`. Raw table:

**2 / 7 PASS guard-off** (EDU, E43F). All five failures are TP.

The decisive comparison, cell by cell (off → on):

| cell | worstWin | whole | TP | kept ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 0.577 → 1.260 | −16.2 → −16.8 | +0.9 → −2.4 | −3.70 |
| 56F1 | 0.558 → 0.687 | −16.1 → −16.2 | −0.6 → −1.6 | −2.20 |
| 4D36 | 0.328 → 0.328 | −16.1 → −16.1 | −0.1 → −0.1 | −1.50 |
| EDU | 0.176 → 0.176 | −16.0 → −16.0 | −1.2 → −1.2 | −1.50 |
| 6A58 | 0.453 → 0.593 | −16.1 → −16.2 | −0.6 → −1.6 | −2.20 |
| E43F | 0.288 → 0.288 | −16.0 → −16.0 | −1.0 → −1.0 | −1.50 |
| BIG | 0.414 → 1.863 | −16.2 → −17.4 | +1.5 → −0.6 | −4.30 |

Two OBSERVED readings, and I want them separated because they point in different directions:

1. **The new output stage alone clears every loudness gate on all 7 cells** — worst-window
   0.176–0.577 against the 0.9 bar, whole-program error 0.0 to −0.2 LU against the 0.5 bar. Every
   loudness failure in §81 is produced by the guard's ceiling drop, and it scales with the drop.
2. **The nominal −1.5 dBTP stage does not reach the −1.0 dBTP emitted gate** — 5 of 7 cells breach
   it guard-off. So the guard is load-bearing for TP and destructive of loudness at the same time.

**INFERRED (not instrumented):** the loop converges its gain curve by measuring at the −1.50 dBTP
stage; the guard then re-emits at a ceiling the loop never measured; the extra limiter gain reduction
lowers the emitted loudness and widens the window spread. The observed scaling is consistent with
this; I did not instrument the loop to prove it.

**The incumbent breaches too — +2.8 dBTP, 4 frames over 0 dBFS.** Confirms the order's conditional:

**Decision 2, the stage.** `aresample=192000:resampler=soxr:precision=28,alimiter=limit=0.841395:attack=5:release=50:asc=0:level=0,aresample=48000:resampler=soxr:precision=28`.
**soxr is present in the production build** and was used for every number in this part. (OBSERVED)

**Decision 3, the guard.** 5 triggers / 7 cells, 379.4 s extra wall, 2 WARNINGs (both quoted in §81).
Per-attempt detail is in §23.3 of the questions file. **The loop is not monotone:** on 4D36 a 1.2 dB
lower ceiling (−1.50 → −2.70) produced a **+0.30 dB higher** emitted peak (−0.10 → +0.20 dBTP). The
product `tp_guard` handled this correctly (it kept the lower measurement), but one re-emit does not
reach compliance on either hot cell. (OBSERVED; mechanism INFERRED/unexplained)

**Decision 5, the Q13 follow-up** (`python q13b.py --only BIG --reps 3 --limits=-1.5,guard` →
`out/run_q13b.log`):

```

BIG   ceiling -1.50 dBTP: encodes=3 with_a_finding=0 peak_of_peaks=-0.142 dBFS
```

**Expected 0, measured 0 of 6**, at both ceilings, byte-identical per ceiling, zero decode errors.
The round-5 artifact still carries 1 frame at +4.426 dBFS. (OBSERVED)

**Decision 6, the Q10 summed-curve slew clamp.** Implemented in `cell5c.py` (sum-slew after
`combine`). Full 7-cell re-run → `out/run_5c_q10.log`, `out/cells_q10.json`. **Every gating number is
identical to §81.** A diff of the two JSONs shows only wall-clock jitter plus two new fields,
`"re_slewed": true` and `"sum_max_rate_db_per_s_after_reslew": 0.5`, on exactly the two long cells
(4D36, BIG). The clamp engaged, found the summed rate already at 0.5000 dB/s, and changed nothing —
`trim` is a constant offset so `|d(curve+trim)/dt| = |dcurve/dt|`, which the per-cell slew already
bounds. **Done, and inert.** (OBSERVED)

- **Did not re-apply `preparer_hunks.patch`** (it still applies — `FORWARD_OK`).
- **Did not commit or stage anything.**
- **Did not choose Q15.** Filed as a product question in `questions/U25.md` §24.
- **Could not verify the guard's loudness mechanism by instrumenting the loop** — §83 point 2 is
  INFERRED from the off/on delta, not measured inside the loop.
- **Could not explain 4D36's 156.6 s re-emit** in the §81 run (4.8× the same work). It did not
  reproduce in the Q10 re-run (29.82 s) and it changes no gate outcome, but it is unexplained and I
  am not claiming it away.

  other six cells were not rescanned at sample level.

  guard-free measurement plus an arithmetic addition.

## 87. Doubts

1. **The panel result is robust but the design is not mine to fix.** Both configurations fail, for
   opposite reasons (§83). I can measure the trade-off; choosing which side to spend is the
   coordinator's call. I have not written code toward either answer.
2. **The stop hinged on a 0.3 dB tolerance and 4D36 missed by 0.90 dB.** If the coordinator's intent
   was "any TP-only failure within a small margin is acceptable", then 0.90 dB is outside it by a
   wide margin and the stop stands as written. I re-read the sentence twice; I do not think it is
   ambiguous, but it is the single closest call in this round and I am flagging it.
3. **The guard's non-monotonicity worries me more than the loudness regression.** A control loop
   whose output moves the wrong way when you tighten its input is not a knob I would ship as the
   primary TP control, whatever ceiling the panel settles on. That is Q15a.
4. **`emitted.q12.ts` was not byte-reproducible** across rounds (md5 recorded in `out/run_q14det.log`;
   `emitted.r5.ts` md5 `55042491c52f4d55f2d0adb3aead4417` vs `emitted.q12.ts`
   `208fab813961bcae179898f98647fc0c`, both 8999.9 s, sizes 271630108 vs 271602284). The ride's PCM
   route *is* byte-deterministic (`P1` = `P2` = `31b2ab2478f77f572cca9af05f000919`), so the drift is
   downstream of the ride. I did not chase it. It does not affect any gate outcome, but it means the
   round-5 emitted artifacts are not a stable comparison baseline.
5. **A cell5b print bug** shows the *last* attempt's `emitted` in the per-cell `guard:` line, not the
   *kept* attempt's: 4D36 prints `emitted=0.2` where the kept value is −0.10. The table and JSON use
   the kept value; only the inline print is misleading. Not fixed, since the harness is not product.
