## 90. Decision 1 — the encoder lever, measured

### 90a. The prep profile, as used by the station's builder (OBSERVED)

`civiccast/egress/models.py:129-132`:

```python
audio_codec: str = "aac"
audio_bitrate_kbps: int = 192
audio_sample_rate: int = 48000
audio_channels: int = 2
```

and asserted from the real builder rather than read off the model, by `cell7.py` at startup:

```text
audio codec flags (asserted from the builder): ['-c:a', 'aac', '-b:a', '192k', '-ar', '48000', '-ac', '2']
```

**So the order's two columns collapse into one.** "The current audio bitrate" *is* 192 kbps; the shipped
profile already emits there. The informative extra columns are therefore 256 (ordered) and, as a
labelled supplement, 320 and 384.

### 90b. The lever, per cell and bitrate

Every row below is quoted from `out/abr_dec1_small.json`, `out/abr_dec1_big.json` and
`out/abr_dec1_320.json` (the supplement), all emitted from the new stage at the nominal −1.5 dBTP
ceiling with the guard OFF — the same guard-off state as round 6's decisive control, so the only
variable between columns is the audio bitrate. `TP` is the seek-free whole-file `ebur128=peak=true`
measurement; `over0` is decoded samples above 0 dBFS from `scan_ts.scan`.

| cell | kbps | reps | emitted I | LRA | emitted TP | over0 | peak max dBFS | peak frame | bytes | eff. kbps | hard <= 0 | target <= -1.0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 192 | 3 | -16.2 | 13.4 | **-0.20** | 0 | -0.911 | 391 | 13,430,344 | 240.9 | ok | miss |
| EF4 | 256 | 3 | -16.2 | 13.4 | **-1.10** | 0 | -1.198 | 394 | 16,590,060 | 297.5 | ok | **met** |
| 4D36 | 192 | 3 | -16.1 | 5.4 | **-0.30** | 0 | -0.478 | 53814 | 54,334,068 | 241.5 | ok | miss |
| 4D36 | 256 | 3 | -16.1 | 5.4 | **-0.60** | 0 | -0.554 | 353 | 67,221,844 | 298.8 | ok | miss |
| 4D36 | 320 * | 2 | -16.0 | 5.4 | **-0.90** | 0 | -1.100 | 61004 | 84,027,540 | 373.5 | ok | miss |
| 4D36 | 384 * | 2 | -16.0 | 5.4 | **+0.30** | **1** | **+0.265** | 353 | 100,109,248 | 444.9 | **FAIL** | miss |
| BIG | 192 | 2 | -16.2 | 5.3 | **+1.80** | **2** | **+1.830** | 277901 | 271,632,176 | 241.5 | **FAIL** | miss |
| BIG | 256 | 2 | -16.1 | 5.3 | **+0.20** | **1** | **+0.158** | 302630 | 335,781,160 | 298.5 | **FAIL** | miss |
| BIG | 320 * | 2 | -16.1 | 5.3 | **-0.20** | 0 | -0.225 | 100437 | 419,222,140 | 372.6 | ok | miss |
| BIG | 384 * | 2 | -16.1 | 5.3 | **-0.70** | 0 | -0.719 | 179255 | 497,020,488 | 441.8 | ok | miss |

`*` = beyond the order's two columns, emitted as a labelled supplement because 320 kbps was the only
remaining candidate that could have cleared BIG's *hard* gate. The ordered columns are 192 and 256.

EF4's offending frame at 192 is frame 391 at t ~ 8.15 s; BIG's two clipping samples at 192 are
t=5928.555 s (+1.83 dBFS) and t=6914.475 s (+0.596 dBFS). `n_decode_error_lines` = 0 in all 28 rows.

### 90c. Decision 1's authorizing condition is not met, so no bitrate change is authorized

The order's rule: *"If a higher bitrate brings all three to <= -1.0 dBTP, raise the prep profile's
audio bitrate to the lowest bitrate that does."* Measured, it does not:

- **4D36 never reaches <= -1.0 dBTP.** Best is -0.90 at 320 kbps. At 256 it is -0.60.
- **BIG never reaches <= -1.0 dBTP either, and fails the hard gate outright at both ordered
  columns**: +1.80 dBTP / 2 samples over at 192, +0.20 dBTP / 1 sample over at 256.
- EF4 is the one cell a raise fixes (-0.20 -> -1.10 at 256 kbps).

**The lever is also non-monotone, and 384 kbps makes it worse rather than better.** 4D36 improves
monotonically 192 -> 256 -> 320 (-0.30, -0.60, -0.90) and then collapses at 384 to **+0.30 dBTP with one
decoded sample over 0 dBFS** — worse than the shipped 192. The sample that clips at 384 is on frame
**353**, the same transient that is 4D36's worst peak at 256 (frame 353, -0.554 dBFS) and 320
(frame 61004, -1.100 dBFS): one transient in that asset, handled differently by each encoder
configuration. BIG's improvement is decelerating (+1.80, +0.20, -0.20, -0.70, i.e. -1.6, -0.4, -0.5 dB
per 64 kbps step), so extrapolating to <= -1.0 dBTP would take roughly two to three further steps
(~512-576 kbps) — and the 4D36 result at 384 is direct evidence that extrapolating this lever is not
sound.

**No prep-profile bitrate change follows from decision 1, so no cache key changes either.** The key
that *would* have changed, had a raise been authorized, is `EgressPreparer._cache_key()`
(`civiccast/egress/preparer.py:423-455`): it already folds in
`config.canonical_profile.model_dump_json()`, so raising `audio_bitrate_kbps` moves the key by itself;
`_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` (`preparer.py:2253`) is the second, independent lever. The second bitrate
read site is `civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py:752` (`f"{profile.audio_bitrate_kbps}k"`).

### 90d. "One constant" is genuinely one constant (checked, non-finding)

I suspected a raise would have to chase several literals, since `audio_bitrate_kbps=192` appears five
times in `civiccast/egress/headend.py`. It does not. `list_headend_profiles()` printed seven profiles:
`comcast-mtd-hd` ac3 384, `comcast-mtd-sd` ac3 192, `generic-udp-spts` ac3 192,
`harmonic-spectrum-ts` ac3 192, `leightronix-file-drop` ac3 192, `telvue-hypercaster-ip` ac3 192 —
six of them **AC-3** headend presets built by explicit `CanonicalProfile(...)` calls — and
`local-rehearsal-hls`, the only AAC profile, which takes the module default at `models.py:130`
(`headend.py:323`). So the five `192` literals in `headend.py` sit on the AC-3 headend path, not on the
lever decision 1 is about. The lever would have been one constant.

## 91. Determinism: emitted TP is byte-reproducible per input (and why that matters here)

Every cell x bitrate pair above reproduced **byte-identically** across its reps — EF4 and 4D36 at 3
reps each for 192/256, BIG at 2, the supplement at 2 — agreeing to 0.00 dB in emitted TP, to the same
peak frame index, and to the same byte count. This is the control round 6 lacked when EF4's recorded
+0.90 dBTP refused to reproduce as -0.2: the difference there was between two *different* curves (4 dp
vs 5 dp, a 5e-5 dB input change), not between two runs of the same bytes. Combined with the earlier
`sens.py` result — a 5e-5 dB curve change moved EF4's emitted TP by 0.5 dB and moved the offending
frame from 391 to 19908 — the picture is: **emitted TP is deterministic for a fixed input and
hypersensitive between inputs.** A single emitted-TP number is therefore a sound basis for a gate on the
production path, where the input is fixed, and an unsound basis for comparing across slightly different
renders.

## 92. The re-converging guard works end to end (EF4, from the crash-1 archive)

The panel's first cell ran to completion before a defect in my own print statement stopped the run (§93).
Its raw log, `out/run_cell7.crash1.log`:

```text
[EF4] round 0 @ -1.50 dBTP: emitted I=-16.2 TP=0.9 over0=1 peakmax=0.909 worstWin=0.577 wholeErr=-0.2 conv=True iters=1 wall=58.7s
[EF4] guard: +0.90 dBTP emitted -> re-converging at -3.70 dBTP
[EF4] iter 1: trim0=0.200 slope_used=1.000 trim1=1.400 I_C=-16.4 err=-0.4 worst_abs=0.860 wall=17.3s
[EF4] iter 2: trim0=-0.400 slope_used=0.667 trim1=1.550 I_C=-16.0 err=0.0 worst_abs=0.224 wall=22.7s
[EF4] round 1 @ -3.70 dBTP: emitted I=-16.1 TP=-2.8 over0=0 peakmax=-2.849 worstWin=0.304 wholeErr=-0.1 conv=True iters=2 wall=67.9s
```

- Round 0 at the nominal -1.50 dBTP emitted **+0.90 dBTP with 1 sample over 0 dBFS** (worst window 0.577,
  whole -0.2 — its *loudness* was fine; it was the true peak that was hot).
- `guard_next_ceiling` computed **-3.70 dBTP**: -1.50 - (0.90 - (-1.0)) - 0.3 = -1.50 - 1.90 - 0.30,
  the order's formula exactly.
- Round 1 **re-ran the convergence loop** at that ceiling — curve, gap cap, slew gate, trim, two iterations
  (trim1 1.550) — and emitted **I = -16.1, TP = -2.8 dBTP, 0 samples over, worst window 0.304, whole -0.1**.
- Selection kept round 1 (`kept_limit_dbtp -3.70`), `target_met: true`, `guard_warning: null`, cell passes.

This is precisely what round 6's guard got wrong: round 6 re-emitted at a ceiling the loop had never
measured and destroyed EF4's loudness (its guard-ON panel failed EF4's loudness gate). Re-converging at
the new ceiling instead *improves* the loudness margin here — worst window 0.577 -> 0.304.

## 93. A defect in my own round-7 harness — found, fixed, and fixed verifiably

The panel died after cell 1 with `TypeError: must be real number, not str`. Cause: the per-cell DONE
print in `cell7.py` passed **16 arguments into a 15-placeholder format string**, so
`warm=%.1fs/%s` consumed the budget and the following `%.1f` received the string `"PASS"`. Evidence,
preserved: `out/run_cell7.crash1.log` and `out/cells_r7.crash1.json`.

Nothing measured before the crash is invalidated: `run_cell7` writes the cell's JSON *before* the print,
so EF4's full result (the §92 table) survived, and the crash was in a display line, not in a measurement.

Fixed by extracting the line into `done_line(rep)` and checking it against the real EF4 JSON on disk
before re-running, in **both** branch states:

```text
[EF4] DONE kept=round1@-3.70 TP=-2.8 over0=0 worstWin=0.304 whole=-0.1 passes=True wall=126.6s (nominal 58.7s + guard 67.9s) warm=126.6s/300.0s PASS ondemand=126.6s/300s PASS
[EF4] DONE kept=round1@-3.70 TP=-2.8 over0=0 worstWin=0.304 whole=-0.1 passes=True wall=126.6s (nominal 58.7s + guard 67.9s) warm=126.6s/300.0s FAIL ondemand=126.6s/300s FAIL
arity/mapping OK for both branch states
```

Two further defects in this same harness I found and fixed before it first ran, both recorded here
because they were real and both would have corrupted a gate:

1. `converge_round` re-ran `validate.measure_whole` on bytes `run_cell`'s `emitted_report` had already
   measured. Same function, same bytes: no independence, one extra full decode per round, and it would
   have inflated the timing budget this panel has to report.
2. The driver initially carried only the warm timing budget. Decision 3 says *"Timing: both gates as
   before"*; the on-demand budget (a flat 300 s) was missing. Both are now in the `timing` dict, with
   the guard's incremental cost broken out separately.

## 94. Machine contention — a caveat that applies to every wall-clock number in this part

The box was **not idle** during this round. Sampled at panel start:

```text
CPU load %: 97
session-0 ffmpeg (the live station): 3-4 processes, publishing
```

and EF4's nominal round measured **58.7 s** here against **21.8 s** for the same cell, converged in the
same 1 iteration, in round 6's control run (`cells_q12.guardoff_all.json`: `cell_wall=21.79`, its
`iter 1 wall=9.07 s`; round 7's `iter 1 wall=36.5 s`). I did not record the load during the control run,
so I cannot attribute the difference — I can only report that walls this round are ~2.7x the control's
for identical work.

What this does and does not affect:

- **Gate outcomes (loudness, true peak) are unaffected** — they are measurements of emitted audio, not of
  time.
- **The guard's incremental cost is unaffected** — it is a within-run differential, nominal round and
  guard round measured back-to-back under the same load.
- **Pass/fail against the two wall-clock budgets is affected**, and the round-6 control's own budgets were
  also measured under unrecorded load. If a cell fails a timing budget in §95, that number is a floor on
  the true cost, not a property of the change alone.

## 96. Determinism: round 0 reproduces the round-6 guard-off control exactly, in all 7 cells

The decisive control for this panel is round 6's guard-off run (`out/cells_q12.guardoff_all.json`). Round
7's round 0 is, by construction, the same measurement: nominal ceiling, guard off, same stage. Measured:

```text
cell        ctrl_TP     r7_r0_TP    delta ctrl_iters    same?
EF4             0.9          0.9     0.00          1      YES
56F1           -0.6         -0.6     0.00          1      YES
4D36           -0.1         -0.1     0.00          2      YES
EDU            -1.2         -1.2     0.00          1      YES
6A58           -0.6         -0.6     0.00          1      YES
E43F           -1.0         -1.0     0.00          1      YES
BIG             1.5          1.5     0.00          2      YES
```

Every cell agrees to 0.00 dB **and** to the same convergence iteration count (1, 1, 2, 1, 1, 1, 2). This
matters because it closes the hole §91 opened: the panel's verdict does not depend on which run you read
— the emitted true peak for a fixed input is reproducible, and the only variable between the control and
this panel is the guard, which is exactly what decision 2 changed.

## 97. The guard's incremental cost, measured per cell

Decision 2 orders the guard's cost measured, on the theory that a guard round reuses the decoded video and
so costs only the audio ride + AAC + mux. `cell7.py` reports the guard's cost as the wall of the rounds
after the nominal one — a within-run differential, nominal and guard rounds measured back-to-back under
the same load:

| cell | nominal round | guard rounds | total | guard share |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | 21.9 s | 30.4 s | 52.2 s | 58% |
| 56F1 | 78.8 s | 80.5 s | 159.3 s | 51% |
| 4D36 | 121.5 s | 162.0 s | 283.6 s | 57% |
| 6A58 | 265.8 s | 133.7 s | 399.5 s | 33% |
| BIG | 657.4 s | **987.6 s** | 1645.0 s | **60%** |

**This is an upper bound on the shipped guard's cost, not the shipped cost.** The panel harness re-decodes
the source and re-runs the whole loop per round; the product design decision 2 specifies reuses the decoded
video and the mux, so the implemented guard round should cost strictly less than the number above. I have
not measured the implemented design's cost, because the design is not implemented. What the table does
establish is that the guard is not free: it is one more full round of the same work, and on BIG that round
is 987.6 s.

## 98. The timing column is not the gate round 6 defined — and that is my harness's defect

Decision 3 says *"Timing: both gates as before, now including the guard's cost on the cells where it
triggers."* **"As before" refers to round 6's measurement, and round 6 measured the timing gates with
`timing5.py` on the product path, not with the panel.** Quoted from `reports/U25.md` §85
(`out/run_timing_answer6.log`):

```text
[BIG] video=797.0s ride=121.9s mux=64.2s total=983.2s (0.1092 s/s) budget=1688s -> PASS headroom=1.72x  prio(video)=BELOW_NORMAL
[ONDEMAND] video=82.4s ride=24.1s mux=2.5s total=109.0s (0.0606 s/s) budget=300s -> PASS headroom=2.75x  prio(video)=BELOW_NORMAL
```

and the gap §85 already names: *"`timing5.py` does not run the guard; the on-demand gate with the guard is
an arithmetic sum of two measured numbers (109.03 + the Q10 4D36 guard cost 29.82 s = 138.85 s vs 300 s),
labelled INFERRED, not measured end-to-end."*

The column in my §95 table is a different quantity. The panel harness is **audio-only**:

- `r5/ride.py:403` — the decoder args carry `-vn` (`["-hide_banner","-loglevel","error","-ss",inpoint,
  "-i",src,"-t",dur,"-vn","-ar","48000","-ac","2","-f","f32le","-"]`): no video is decoded at all.
- `r5/ride.py:435` — the encode sink is an audio-only MPEG-TS (`[... "-f","f32le","-ar","48000","-ac","2",
  "-i","pipe:0","-af","volume=..,<limiter>", *codec, "-f","mpegts", out]` with
  `codec = ["-c:a","aac","-b:a","192k","-ar","48000","-ac","2"]`).

So `cell_wall_s` is source-decode + N ride passes + AAC/MPEG-TS write + measurement, and it is not
comparable to a budget whose reference measurement (`timing5.py`) is dominated by a video transcode — 797.0
of BIG's 983.2 s. **My "5/7 timing" row is therefore not evidence that decision 3's timing gate fails.** It
is a pessimistic audio-only diagnostic that I put next to whole-path budgets; the harness defect is mine,
and §94's contention caveat applies to it.

Two consequences worth stating plainly, both INFERRED arithmetic on measured components, neither measured
end-to-end:

- **BIG, product path, with the guard**: 983.2 s + (ride 121.9 + mux 64.2, the video reused) = **1169.3 s**
  against 1688 s → **PASS, 1.44×**.
- **BIG's on-demand miss is pre-existing and guard-independent**: the *control* run took **775.4 s**
  against the 300 s on-demand budget with no guard at all. BIG is the 2.5-hour warm cell; the flat 300 s
  budget is the on-demand path's, and applying it to BIG was my table's construction, not round 6's.
- **BIG's warm gate passed this round**: 1645.0 / 1687.5 = 0.975, *including* the guard's 987.6 s.
- **6A58 at control speed**: 79.65 s nominal (its control) + 133.72 s guard = **213.4 s**, inside both the
  337.5 s warm and 300 s on-demand budgets.

**The honest statement of the gap: I have not measured the product-path timing gate with the guard
enabled.** §85 measured it guard-free; decision 3 asks for it guard-included; the number I have for the
guard-included product path is an arithmetic addition of two measured pieces, exactly as §23.5 did.

## 99. The 6A58 / E43F wall anomaly, localized

Round 7's round-0 walls do not track the control uniformly. Factored against round 6's control:

```text
cell    ctrl_wall  r7_r0wall  factor guard_wall      total warm_bud  ondem_bud
EF4         21.79      21.87    1.00      30.37      52.24    300.0      300.0
56F1        80.78      78.84    0.98      80.45     159.29    337.5      300.0
4D36       118.57     121.54    1.03     162.02     283.56    337.5      300.0
EDU         91.91      93.15    1.01       0.00      93.15    337.5      300.0
6A58        79.65     265.80    3.34     133.72     399.52    337.5      300.0
E43F        82.86     185.78    2.24       0.00     185.78    337.5      300.0
BIG        775.40     657.41    0.85     987.59    1645.00   1687.5      300.0
```

The anomaly is not extra loop work. The convergence-loop walls are near-identical, and the iteration counts
are identical:

```text
cell   ctrl_iters   r0_iters ctrl_iter_wall   r0_iter_wall
EF4             1          1           9.07           9.97
56F1            1          1          37.27          35.55
4D36            2          2           74.0          74.95
EDU             1          1          37.15          38.08
6A58            1          1          35.96          39.17
E43F            1          1          35.62          68.06
BIG             2          2         533.36         430.46
```

Subtracting the loop leaves the encode + measure stages, and that is where the outliers live: 6A58's
non-loop time is **226.6 s against the control's 43.7 s (5.19×)**, E43F's is 117.7 s against 47.2 s
(2.49×), while the other five reproduce within 6% (0.94×–1.05×). Both outliers used **one** loop iteration
in both runs and produced **byte-identical** results (a −0.6 dBTP and a −1.0 dBTP artifact, §96). So the
extra seconds bought no work: this is a load/stall artifact in the encode+measure stages, of the same
family as §94's contention and §86's unexplained 4D36 156.6 s. I cannot attribute the cause, and I am not
claiming it away; I state only that it is outside the loop, that the artifacts are identical, and that no
gate outcome depends on it.

## 100. What I did NOT do, and what I could not verify

- **Did not implement the ride in `preparer.py`.** `preparer.py` is at HEAD; `loudness_ride.py` is still
  untracked and unwired. **No commit, no stage, no push, no tag.**
- **Did not re-apply `preparer_hunks.patch`.**
- **Did not choose Q15**, and did not treat answer 7 as closing it.
- **Did not measure the product-path timing gate with the guard enabled** (§98). This is the gap that
  blocks a clean decision-4 verdict, and it is a measurement I can make if the coordinator says the
  product path is the gate.
- **Did not measure the shipped guard's cost** — §97 is the panel's cost, which is an upper bound.
- **Did not re-verify frames-over-zero outside the kept artifact**; the guard rounds' discarded artifacts
  were deleted by `cell7.py` after selection.
- **Could not attribute the 6A58/E43F non-loop slowdown** to any cause (§99).
- **The box was loaded throughout**: CPU load 97% and 3–4 session-0 station ffmpeg at panel start. Every
  wall-clock number in this part is a floor on the true cost, as §94 says.

## 101. Doubts

1. **The decision-4 branch is ambiguous, and I did not pick a branch.** Decision 4's implement condition
   is "every cell passes loudness + hard TP **and both timing gates pass**". Measured: 7/7 on loudness +
   hard TP; 5/7 on the panel's timing column. Under round 6's own accounting (§98) both timing gates pass
   with the guard. Neither reading is mine to choose, so I stopped and filed the question
   (`questions/U25.md` §26).
2. **The guard's trigger rate is high**: 5 of 7 cells, including two (56F1 at −0.6, 4D36 at −0.1) whose
   nominal overshoot is small. That is a consequence of the encoder's post-AAC overshoot (§90b), not of
   the loop, and it means the guard is closer to the common path than to an exception path. Decision 2
   Q15b fixed the nominal ceiling at −1.5 dBTP; if the guard's cost matters, that ceiling is the lever.
3. **BIG's guard ceiling, −4.30 dBTP, is 2.8 dB below the nominal.** The loop converged there and cleared
   both loudness gates (worst window 0.705 of 0.9), but the margin is thinner than the nominal's. A
   different asset could converge at the guard ceiling and *fail* loudness, in which case `select_leveled_attempt`
   keeps the nominal — the correct behaviour, but it leaves that cell over the target with a WARNING.
4. **Whether "both timing gates pass" was ever meant to be read off the panel at all.** §85 already
   flagged that the on-demand gate was never measured with the guard; answer 7 asked for the panel to
   include the guard's timing cost. If the intent is the panel column, then 6A58 and BIG fail and no branch
   of decision 4 fires — which is exactly the case I am surfacing rather than resolving.

# Part 8 — answer 8: the product path measured, and the branch

## 103. Decision 1 — the product-path timing gate, measured

**What was asked.** Answer 8 decision 1: *"Q16: the product path governs. Measure it … `timing5.py`-style,
the real preparer path with video, at BELOW_NORMAL with the station live, WITH the re-converging guard, for
(a) BIG as the warm 2.5 h asset against 1687.5 s and (b) the 30-minute on-demand segment against 300 s,
choosing for (b) the panel cell whose guard cost was highest among the 30-minute cells (say which). A guard
round must re-run only the audio ride + AAC + mux, reusing the conformed video (answer 7 section 2); if the
product path cannot do that, make it do that, then measure."*

**Cell (b), named: `4D36`** — the sustain source at inpoint 2809.997 s, duration 1800.022 s. It is the
highest-guard-cost 30-minute cell in round 7's panel: its guard added **162.02 s**, against 6A58's 133.72,
56F1's 80.45, EF4's 30.37 and 0.00 for EDU and E43F (those are the `guard_wall` column of §99's table).
Cell (a) is BIG, 9000 s from inpoint 0.

**Harness, and why its numbers mean what I say they mean.** `%TEMP%\u25\r8\timing8.py` (447 lines, nothing
written into the repo). It builds the real prep profile from the live builder bits and prints them back —
`builder bits: {"vf": "scale=1280:720:force_original_aspect_ratio=decrease,pad=1280:720:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2,fps=30,format=yuv420p", "codec": "h264_mf", "b_v": "6000k", "gop": "60", "audio": ["-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "192k", "-ar", "48000", "-ac", "2"], "fg_threads": 8}`
— then runs the S series pass, the V video pass, and per round `lr.converge()` + **one** real emit
(`build_encode_sink_args`: AAC/MPEG-TS at 192 kbps) + a seek-free measurement of the emitted bytes, driving
the guard through the shipped `lr.guard_next_ceiling` and `lr.select_leveled_attempt`. The guard round
re-runs **only** the ride loop + AAC + mux; the conformed video is reused for the final mux, which is
exactly what answer 7 §2 requires. Run at BELOW_NORMAL (the harness prints its own class:
`self priority class: BELOW_NORMAL`) with the station live, and the ride pinned to the station's own ffmpeg
(`ride binary: C:\Program Files\CivicCast (Native)\dependencies\ffmpeg\bin\ffmpeg.exe`).

**Column definitions, read off the harness so these numbers are not mistaken for something else**
(`timing8.py:312, 354–356, 405–407`):

| column | definition | what it is |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `total_wall_s` | `V + S + Σ round_wall_s + M` | the product path **with** the guard |
| `total_no_guard_wall_s` | `V + S + round[0] + M` | the same path with the guard's extra rounds removed |
| `total_timing5_shape_wall_s` | `V + round[0].emit + M` | round 6's *accounting shape*: V + **one** emit + mux. It contains **no ride at all**, so it is not round 6's total and is not compared with one below. |
| `round_wall_s` | `converge + emit + measure` | one guard round's cost |

Budgets are the ones answer 8 names: BIG **1687.5 s** (`max(300, 9000/8 × 1.5)`), on-demand **300.0 s**.

### 103.1 Measured result

```text

[ONDEMAND] V video=236.0s rc=0 prio=BELOW_NORMAL bytes=655567468
```

| case | V | S | rounds | (guard) | M | **total (guard)** | budget | ratio | gate | no-guard | gate | r6-shape |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4D36 on-demand | 236.0 | 14.6 | 281.8 | 161.5 | 1.6 | **534.0** | 300.0 | **1.78×** | **FAIL** | 372.5 | **FAIL** | 265.7 |
| BIG warm-2.5h | 906.0 | 151.0 | 2857.5 | 1543.1 | 24.3 | **3938.9** | 1687.5 | **2.33×** | **FAIL** | 2395.8 | **FAIL** | 1199.8 |

**Quality, both cells: PASS.** 4D36 kept round 1 at the guard ceiling −2.70 dBTP with `I=-16.0`, `TP=-1.4`
dBTP, worst window 0.255 of the 0.9 LU tolerance, whole-program error 0.0 LU, `over0=0`. BIG kept round 1 at
its guard ceiling −4.10 dBTP with `I=-16.0`, `TP=-1.3` dBTP, worst window 0.349, whole-program error 0.0 LU,
`over0=0`. The loudness + hard-TP gate is not what fails here; the timing gate is.

**Both gates fail, and neither failure is a near-miss:** 4D36 at **1.78×** and BIG at **2.33×**, with the
guard-free counterfactuals still over (1.24× and 1.42×). BIG's guard-free column is itself decisive for a
reason that does not depend on the guard at all: V 906.0 + S 151.0 = 1057.0 s of the 1687.5 s budget, leaving
630.5 s, and BIG's **nominal** round took **1314.4 s** (978.3 + 269.5 + 66.6).

## 104. Where the on-demand time goes — and what the guard costs

The round-6 control for this same cell is `r5/out/timing.json`: `warm-2.5h` (BIG) `video_wall_s 797.04`,
`ride_wall_s 121.94`, `mux_wall_s 64.19`, `total_wall_s 983.17`, `video_bytes 3440063856`; and
`ondemand-30min` `video_wall_s 82.41`, `ride_wall_s 24.13`, `mux_wall_s 2.49`, `total_wall_s 109.03`,
`video_bytes 655567468`. Decomposing 109.03 → 534.0:

| component | round 6 control | today | delta |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| V video | 82.41 | 236.0 | **+153.6** |
| S series | — (not a timing5 stage) | 14.6 | +14.6 |
| rounds (ride + emit + measure) | 24.13 (one ride) | 281.8 | **+257.7** |
| M mux | 2.49 | 1.6 | −0.9 |
| **total** | **109.03** | **534.0** | **+425.0** |

Two independent causes, and **each one alone fails the 300 s gate**:

- **With the guard's rounds but round-6's V speed**: 82.4 + 14.6 + 281.8 + 1.6 = **380.4 s → FAIL**.
- **With today's V but no guard**: **372.5 s → FAIL**.
- **Guard-free at round-6 V speed** (the only combination that passes): 82.4 + 14.6 + 120.2 + 1.6 =
  **218.8 s → PASS 1.37×**.

So the gate is not a near-miss with one culprit. It fails on the product path's *own per-round cost* and it
fails again on the video stage's non-reproducibility, independently.

**Why a guard round costs 161.5 s — a code fact, OBSERVED.** `converge()` renders **three times per
iteration**: `ride_only = render(applied, None)` (`loudness_ride.py:1121`), `with_trim0 = render(applied, trim0)`
(`:1129`), `with_trim1 = render(applied, trim1)` (`:1135`), inside `for iteration in range(1, params.max_iters + 1)`
(`:1120`). Today's 4D36 rounds report `iters=2` and `iters=3`, i.e. **6 and 9 full rides** — matching the
measured converge walls within 1.6%: 85.4 s / 6 = **14.23 s per ride**, 126.0 s / 9 = **14.00 s per ride**. A
guard round then also pays the emit (27.8 s) and the measurement (7.7 s):
126.0 + 27.8 + 7.7 = **161.5 s**, which is the `guard` column exactly.

`timing5.py` modelled a round as **one** ride: its control's `ride_wall_s 24.13` for the same cell, which at
14.1 s/ride is ~1.7 rides. That is the whole "timing5 shape vs product path" gap the coordinator asked to
expose: **the guard round's cost is a second full convergence loop, and the loop is 6–9 rides, not one.**

**104.1 The same arithmetic on BIG, and it scales linearly.** BIG's rounds iterate 3 and 4 times, i.e. **9
and 12 rides**: 978.3 s / 9 = **108.7 s per ride**, 1371.5 s / 12 = **114.3 s per ride** — the same ~14 s per
1800 s of audio as 4D36, scaled by the 5× longer asset. So the product path's per-round cost is
`3 × max_iters × (per-second ride rate)`, and at 9000 s that is **~1.3–1.5 ks per round** against a warm
budget of 1687.5 s. The guard round's own cost on BIG was **1543.1 s**.

One more measured fact worth naming, because it is why the guard fires: BIG's *nominal* attempt emitted
**TP = +1.30 dBTP** — above digital full scale — from a nominal ceiling of −1.5 dBTP, and 4D36's nominal
emitted −0.10 dBTP. The post-AAC overshoot is +2.8 dB and +1.4 dB respectively (§90b's finding, now
confirmed on the full-size asset). The guard is not a rare corrective here; it is the mechanism that brings
an over-full-scale artifact back under the hard bound. And note that the guard's own emit wall was not
stable either: BIG round 0 emitted in 269.5 s and round 1 in 133.1 s, same code, same duration.

## 105. The video stage is not reproducible on this box — at byte-identical output

This is the load evidence behind the +153.6 s, and it is worth stating precisely because it bounds how much
any single-run verdict can mean.

| cell | run | `video_bytes` | V wall | factor |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4D36 (1800 s) | round 6 control | 655,567,468 | 82.41 s | 1.00× |
| 4D36 (1800 s) | round 8 (today) | **655,567,468** | **236.0 s** | **2.86×** |
| BIG (9000 s) | round 5 | — | 503.4 s | 0.63× |
| BIG (9000 s) | round 6 control | 3,440,063,856 | 797.04 s | 1.00× |
| BIG (9000 s) | round 8 (today) | **3,440,063,856** | **906.0 s** | **1.14×** |

The byte counts are **identical to the byte** in both cells. Same source, same inpoint, same duration, same
argv, same binary, same profile: the encoder's output is deterministic and the wall is not. BIG spans
503.4 s → 906.0 s (1.80×) across three runs; 4D36 spans 82.41 s → 236.0 s (2.86×) across two.

**OBSERVED, from a read-only sampler I wrote for this purpose** (`%TEMP%\u25\r8\sample_load.ps1`, sampling
30 s from 16:03:49 to 16:12:24, while BIG's V pass ran):

- My BIG V ffmpeg (PID 22432) burned **5.47–6.19 cores** and wrote 4.9–8.3 MB/s from 16:04 to 16:09
  (`16:08:28  v= 6.790 MB/s … mine= 6.19c  others= 1.10c`, `io pid=22432 ffmpeg#3 r=3,013,317 w=6,860,619`).
- At **16:10:40 it collapsed**: `v= 1.381 MB/s … mine= 1.45c  others= 0.94c` — the same process at
  1.45 cores and 1.38 MB/s, and it stayed at 1.39–2.17 cores / 1.38–2.30 MB/s to the end of the sample.
- Nothing else on the box was using the CPU: `others` never exceeded **1.10 cores** of 16 logical
  processors across all 16 samples. The stall is not CPU contention.
- What was on the I/O path at the same time: the station service python (PID 2380) reading
  **364,728,664 B/s** at `16:07:26`, 230.9 MB/s at `16:07:57`, 225.7 MB/s at `16:09:30`; station ffmpeg
  sessions writing 0.5–3.7 MB/s; and **another agent's `grep.exe` (PID 36608) reading
  `1,158,729,478 B/s`** at `16:07:57` and 147.0 MB/s at `16:09:30` — over the same volume my source lives
  on. `io pid=36608 grep#1 r=1,158,729,478 w=0`.
- The GPU `videoencode` engine averaged **0.00%** (0.00% max) during the run, so `h264_mf` here is a
  software MFT path, not hardware — the encode cannot be blamed on a saturated GPU engine.

**INFERRED, not proven:** the V-stage inflation is disk/IO contention from the live station's own
transcode jobs plus a concurrent agent's recursive `grep` over `C:\ProgramData`, on a box whose CPU was
mostly idle. I cannot attribute it causally from this run — the 4D36 inflation (2.86×) happened *before* the
sampler started, so I have no load trace for it at all. What I can state without inference is the part that
matters for the decision: **the same argv produced the same bytes in 82.41 s and 236.0 s on the same box on
the same day.** A single-run timing verdict on this machine therefore carries up to ~2.9× wall uncertainty,
and a margin thinner than that is not a verdict.

**Caveat on the sampler's per-PID rows.** The perf-instance → PID mapping in `Win32_PerfFormattedData_PerfProc_Process`
is unreliable once a process exits (PID 34696 appeared as `ffmpeg#4` in the 16:10 sample and was `sleep.exe 30`
by 16:12). The aggregate rate and CPU columns are sound; individual `io pid=…` rows are approximate
attribution, which is why I quote them as corroboration of a concurrent load, not as a causal proof.

## 106. Decision 2 — the 192 kbps audio bitrate and the cache key

- **192 kbps is already the shipped default.** OBSERVED: `civiccast/egress/models.py:130`,
  `audio_bitrate_kbps: Annotated[int, Field(gt=0)] = 192`; the profile is consumed at
  `civiccast/egress/preparer.py:2444-2445` (`"-b:a", f"{profile.audio_bitrate_kbps}k"`) and, on the ride's
  encode, at `civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py:752`. The harness's own builder-bits print above confirms the
  live profile's audio tuple is `["-c:a","aac","-b:a","192k","-ar","48000","-ac","2"]` — the same setting the
  7/7 loudness + hard-TP pass was measured at. **No profile edit is required for decision 2.** The one
  non-192 entry in the tree (`civiccast/egress/headend.py:212`, `audio_bitrate_kbps=384`) is inside the
  `comcast-mtd-hd` *headend preset catalogue* — a description of what that headend ingests, not a prep
  profile the preparer encodes with.
- **The key that changes with the leveler is `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION`.** `EgressPreparer._cache_key()`
  (`civiccast/egress/preparer.py:423`) hashes source path/size/mtime, `config.canonical_profile.model_dump_json()`,
  `loudness_target_lufs`, `loudness_tolerance_lufs` and `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` (`:448`). Because the
  bitrate is already 192, `model_dump_json()` does not move; the identity that must move when the ride ships
  is `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION = "loudnorm-v2-twopass"` (`preparer.py:2253`) → `"loudnorm-v3-ride"`. Every
  conformed artifact in the field cache is invalidated by that bump, which is the intent.
- **The encoder/bitrate lever was not monotone and is not relied on.** Recorded here as decision 2 asks;
  the round-4/5 A/B sweeps (§§62–70) found emitted true peak moving non-monotonically with AAC bitrate, so
  no gate in this design rests on the bitrate lever. The 192 kbps figure is kept because it is what the
  7/7 measurement was taken at, not because it buys headroom.

## 107. Decision 3 — the branch: STOP

Answer 8: *"If both product-path timing gates pass: implement exactly as answer 5 + answer 7 section 4 say
… If either fails: STOP with the measured numbers."*

**They do not both pass. Both fail.**

| gate | requirement | measured | verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (b) 4D36 on-demand | ≤ 300.0 s | **534.0 s** (1.78×); 372.5 s guard-free | **FAIL** |
| (a) BIG warm-2.5h | ≤ 1687.5 s | **3938.9 s** (2.33×); 2395.8 s guard-free | **FAIL** |

So the STOP branch is taken. **No implementation was done:** `preparer.py` is untouched at HEAD, no hunk of
`%TEMP%\u25\preparer_hunks.patch` was re-applied, `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION` was **not** bumped,
`civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py` was **not** wired in, no test was written or run, no gate was run, and
nothing was committed, staged, pushed or tagged. `git status --porcelain` in the worktree is still exactly
`?? civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py`; HEAD is still `8e9c2ed7`.

**For the coordinator, the levers this measurement puts on the table** (these are INFERRED options, not
recommendations I have acted on, and each needs a decision):

1. **Make the guard round not re-converge.** A guard round exists to lower the ceiling; the ride curve that
   produced the nominal attempt is already in hand. Re-riding at a lowered ceiling costs one loop, but the
   *loop* is what costs 161.5 s — `converge()` pays 3 renders/iteration to solve for the trim slope. If a
   guard round could re-use the measured slope and re-render once or twice instead of re-converging from
   scratch, its cost would fall by roughly the factor between 6–9 rides and 1–2, i.e. into the on-demand
   headroom (INFERRED from the 14.0–14.2 s/ride measurement, not measured as a whole).
2. **Q15b's nominal ceiling.** The guard triggers when the emitted TP is above −1.0 dBTP; 4D36's nominal
   emitted −0.10 dBTP tripped it and 56F1's −0.6 dBTP tripped it too. The nominal ceiling is fixed at
   −1.5 dBTP by Q15b, so the trigger rate is an artifact of the post-AAC overshoot, not of the loop — but
   the ceiling is the only knob that changes how often the guard is entered. Lowering it trades loudness
   margin for guard rounds.
3. **Budget the guard on the warm path only**, or accept the guard where the budget is loose (BIG's budget
   is 5.6× the on-demand one). This is a product decision about which path must be fast.
4. **Re-measure, on a quieter box, before treating either gate as settled.** Given §105, a 2.86× V-stage
   swing between two runs of identical argv, neither of today's verdicts is stable to better than that
   factor. Today's box was live, and another agent was reading 1.16 GB/s off the same volume for part of it.

## 108. What I did NOT do, and what I could not verify

- **Did not implement anything.** See §107. `preparer.py` at HEAD; `loudness_ride.py` untracked and unwired.
- **Did not re-apply `preparer_hunks.patch`.** OBSERVED: the file is **85 lines, 4,390 bytes**, and its last
  line is `+_LOUDNESS_METHOD_RIDE = "ride"  # noqa: S105 - label, not a secret`. It contains the docstring +
  constants hunk and **no wiring**; the hunks that would call the ride from either preparer path do not exist
  yet and would have to be written before the patch is usable at all.

### 108.1 Two defects in the unshipped module, found while verifying this report

Neither is a reason the gates failed — the gates failed on timing — but both would have to be fixed before
`loudness_ride.py` could ship, and the first is the kind of thing a wiring commit would otherwise inherit
silently.

1. **The module exports the superseded guard, not the measured one.** OBSERVED:
   `civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py:138` `__all__` lists `"tp_guard"` and `"guard_ceiling_dbtp"` — the old
   re-emit-once API (`tp_guard` at `:1290`, `TP_GUARD_MAX_REEMITS = 1` at `:1238`, whose own comment at
   `:1236` reads *"One re-emit. A second would double the cost of the rare hot asset to chase a"*). Answer 7's
   re-converging API — `guard_next_ceiling` (`:1416`) and `select_leveled_attempt` (`:1440`), under
   `TP_GUARD_MAX_ROUNDS = 2` (`:1370`) — is **defined but absent from `__all__`**. So the module currently
   carries **both** guards, and its public contract points at the one answer 7 replaced. The module docstring
   matches that wrong contract: `:94` still reads *"therefore gets the last word (:func:`tp_guard`): after the
   emit, the emitted true peak is measured seek-free, and if it is above ``-1.0 dBTP`` the ride re-emits"*.
   **What this does not change:** the numbers in §103 — `timing8.py` drove `guard_next_ceiling` /
   `select_leveled_attempt` directly, so it measured the answer-7 guard, which is the one the coordinator
   specified. What it does mean is that a future wiring commit must delete or re-point the old guard rather
   than leave two live implementations, and re-write `:1-100` and `__all__` with it.
2. **`preparer_hunks.patch` is truncated** — see the bullet above. Recorded because answer 8's implement
   branch names "re-apply `preparer_hunks.patch`", and the file as it stands cannot be applied to deliver
   the specified behaviour.
- **Did not run the gates** (`pytest tests/egress`, ruff, mypy) — decision 3's STOP branch makes them
  inapplicable, and I did not want to start a suite while another agent's egress suite was intermittently
  present on the box.
- **Did not measure the guard's cost on any cell other than 4D36**; the other six cells' guard walls in §99
  are the round-7 panel's, measured under a different harness.
- **Could not attribute the V-stage inflation** (§105) — no load trace exists for the 4D36 window.
- **Did not verify the emitted artifact beyond the kept round**: the discarded guard artifact was deleted by
  the harness after selection; the kept one is measured and scanned (`over0=0`).
- **The station was live throughout**, and another agent's `grep` and a second worktree's pytest were on the
  box during the run; every wall in this part is a floor on the true cost, and the *spread* is the finding.

## 109. Doubts

1. **The BIG verdict rests on a single run whose video stage was 1.14× its control** — mild, unlike 4D36's
   2.86× — so BIG's number is the more trustworthy of the two, but it is still one run on a shared box.
2. **The guard's trigger rate (5 of 7 panel cells in round 7) makes it near-common-path**, so "the guard
   costs 161.5 s on the on-demand cell" is not an edge case; it is what the shipped design does to that
   path every time the encoder overshoots the nominal ceiling.
3. **I do not know whether the coordinator intends the on-demand gate to include the guard at all.** Answer
   8 says it does, so I measured it that way; but round 6's own passing control excluded it, and §98 already
   flagged that the guard-inclusive on-demand gate had never been measured. It now has been, and it fails.
4. **If the answer is "the guard may be skipped when the budget is tight", the design needs a stated rule**
   for when the shipped product may ship a program whose true peak is above −1.0 dBTP. That is a product
   question (a real overshoot bound vs a wall-clock bound), and I am not inventing an answer to it.

### 29.2 Item 2 — the model-validation gate FAILS

Answer 9's gate, verbatim: *"on all 7 cells, the model's predicted per-window loudness (aligned and
120 s-offset 240 s windows) and whole-program loudness vs the measured emitted artifact: worst
|error| <= 0.15 LU. Report the table."*

The table, best family (`L3 mid`, the dB midpoint of clip and peak-hold), aligned offset, error in LU
vs the emitted artifact's own ebur128 series:

```text
$ .venv/Scripts/python.exe valmodel.py          (r10, ~4 min, reads cached npz/json only)
CELL EF4  dur=446.052 trim=+1.5500 lim=-3.70 C=0.65313 n=4460   art=y lim=y
  L0 none  off=+0.00 | art win=2.247@120.0  whole=1.9789 | lim win=2.18  whole=1.9068
  L1 peak  off=+0.00 | art win=0.758@240.0  whole=-0.6477 | lim win=0.818 whole=-0.7198
  L2 clip  off=+0.00 | art win=1.614@360.0  whole=1.3732 | lim win=1.534 whole=1.3011
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.232@360.0  whole=0.1634 | lim win=0.152 whole=0.0913
CELL 56F1 dur=1800.13 trim=+0.5000 lim=-2.20 C=0.77625 n=18001 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.09@1680.0  whole=0.0528 | lim win=0.02  whole=-0.0013
CELL 4D36 dur=1800.02 trim=+1.8886 lim=-2.70 C=0.73282 n=17999 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.214@720.0  whole=-0.0408 | lim win=0.26 whole=-0.0905
CELL EDU  dur=1800.12 trim=+0.2000 lim=-1.50 C=0.84140 n=18001 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.084@600.0  whole=0.0279 | lim win=0.007 whole=-0.0015
CELL 6A58 dur=1800.08 trim=+0.5000 lim=-2.20 C=0.77625 n=18000 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.103@1680.0 whole=0.0441 | lim win=0.025 whole=-0.0015
CELL E43F dur=1800.04 trim=+0.4000 lim=-1.50 C=0.84140 n=18000 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.063@1320.0 whole=0.0197 | lim win=0.04  whole=0.0152
CELL BIG  dur=9000    trim=+3.2750 lim=-4.30 C=0.60954 n=89996 art=y lim=y
  L3 mid   off=+0.00 | art win=0.225@6840.0 whole=-0.0006 | lim win=0.27 whole=-0.0484
```

**Window leg, 3 of 7 cells over the 0.15 LU budget: EF4 0.232, 4D36 0.214, BIG 0.225.** The other
four clear it (0.090 / 0.084 / 0.103 / 0.063).

**Whole-program leg: EF4 at +0.1634 LU is also over 0.15.** That depends on how answer 9's sentence
scopes — "the model's predicted per-window loudness ... and whole-program loudness vs the measured
emitted artifact: worst |error| <= 0.15 LU" reads naturally as one budget governing both legs, in
which case **4 of 7 cells fail**. In the other reading the whole-program leg is judged against the
product's own ±0.5 LU bar, and only the 3 window failures count. **Both readings are FAIL**; I flag
the ambiguity because §28's question turns on exactly this kind of budget scoping.

Three independent results say this is a form failure, not a tuning miss.

**(a) No single blend parameter works.** OBSERVED, `ascan.py`, `att = att_clip + α·(att_peak − att_clip)`
in dB over α ∈ [0,1]:

```text
alpha     EF4    56F1    4D36     EDU    6A58    E43F     BIG     max
  0.4   0.475   0.199   0.378   0.085   0.216   0.114   0.645   0.645
  0.5   0.232   0.090   0.214   0.084   0.103   0.063   0.224   0.232
  0.6   0.035   0.044   0.586   0.082   0.033   0.046   0.718   0.718
```

The max-over-cells column bottoms out at **α=0.5 → 0.232 LU**. Per-cell optima scatter across
α ∈ [0.5, 0.7] (EF4 0.6→0.035, 56F1 0.6→0.044, **4D36 0.5→0.214**, EDU 0.7→0.080, 6A58 0.6→0.033,
E43F 0.6→0.046, **BIG 0.5→0.224**). Five cells would pass at their own α; **4D36 and BIG are over the
budget at every α in the scan**, at ~1.4–1.5×. A flat minimum would mean the form is right and α is
merely off; a scattered optimum with two cells unpassable anywhere means the form is wrong.

**(b) A free affine law of perfectly-measured inputs does no better.** OBSERVED, `xval.py`,
leave-one-window-out over the three probed windows (`4D36.1300` n=1200, `4D36.700` n=2400, `EF4.206`
n=2400), regressing the true K-domain retention on `[1, att_v2, att_clip, frac_e]`:

```text
--- leave-one-window-out (the honest test) ---
  hold out 4D36.1300  beta=[0.0393, 0.5818, 0.1705, 0.6645] resid p95=1.2917 dB | loud240s worst=0.235 mean=+0.235 LU
  hold out 4D36.700   beta=[0.0172, 0.5739, 0.4327, 0.9908] resid p95=1.7748 dB | loud240s worst=0.019 mean=-0.019 LU
  hold out EF4.206    beta=[0.1051, 0.5857, 0.1772, 0.6318] resid p95=0.7578 dB | loud240s worst=0.206 mean=+0.206 LU
--- per-window fit (in-sample, upper bound) ---
  4D36.1300  loud240s 0.189 LU | 4D36.700 0.142 LU | EF4.206 0.054 LU
--- single-alpha blend, leave-one-window-out ---
  alpha=0.5 worst 240 s window over all probed windows: 0.150 LU
  alpha=0.6 worst 240 s window over all probed windows: 0.503 LU
```

Held-out worst **0.235 / 0.019 / 0.206 LU** — no better than the fixed α=0.5 blend on the same
windows (0.150), and the in-sample fit is an upper bound no real implementation could reach because
the shipped model must also *predict* the ridden peak from the source block plus the curve. The fitted
coefficients are not stationary: `att_v2` holds at 0.57–0.59 but `att_clip` scatters 0.15–0.43 and
`frac_e` scatters 0.58–1.02.

**(c) The mechanism is identified.** OBSERVED, `kdiag.py` — the blend law tracks the limiter's
**unweighted** retention, not the K-domain retention the window loudness needs:

```text
window             n | mean(true-unw)   p95abs   maxabs | mean(mid-true) mean(mid-unw) | corr(mid,true) corr(mid,unw)
4D36.1300       1200 |        +0.1486   1.3355   3.0135 |        -0.1654       -0.0168 |         0.9401        0.9711
4D36.700        2400 |        +0.4863   2.1077   5.0647 |        -0.4965       -0.0103 |         0.9296        0.9820
EF4.206         2400 |        -0.0024   0.5058   1.8201 |        +0.0142       +0.0118 |         0.9389        0.9350
```

`mean(mid − unw)` is −0.0168/−0.0103/+0.0118 dB against `mean(mid − true)` of
−0.1654/−0.4965/+0.0142 dB, and `corr(mid, unw)` (0.971/0.982/0.935) exceeds `corr(mid, true)`
(0.940/0.930/0.939) on every window. The residual is exactly the K-weighted-vs-unweighted gap:
per-block `att_true − att_unw` reaches maxabs **3.01/5.06/1.82 dB**, and energy-weighted
`unw − true` is −0.080/−0.318/−0.007 dB. **OBSERVED conclusion:** `alimiter` is not frequency-flat,
and a law built from unweighted per-block summaries (peak + power histogram) cannot express that.
**INFERRED:** the failing cells are the densely-engaged ones, consistent with `alimiter`'s stateful
5 ms attack / 50 ms release envelope crossing block boundaries — a per-block (peak, histogram)
summary is a lossy view of a filter with memory.

Supporting measurements, for how much room there is: the window is alignment-insensitive (EF4 `L3 mid`
worst window 0.239 / 0.236 / 0.232 LU at gain offsets −0.10 / −0.05 / +0.00), so this is not a
frame-alignment artifact. And on 4D36 and BIG the law is *worse* against the measured limited series
(0.26, 0.27) than against the emitted artifact (0.214, 0.225) — INFERRED, the two errors partially
cancel on those cells; I have not proven that, and it is not a reason to prefer either reference.

---

### 29.3 The one thing that makes this a question rather than a dead end

**OBSERVED.** The model's worst-window error is 0.232 LU. The acceptance bar this unit is judged on is
**±1.0 LU per 240 s window, ±0.5 LU whole program**. The model is off by under a quarter of the window
budget and under a third of the whole-program budget: accurate enough that an artifact converged on it
lands inside the delivered bar, but not accurate enough to *prove* that analytically — which is what
answer 9's 0.15 LU budget asked for, five to seven times tighter than the delivery bar.

Answer 9 anticipated this and named its own remedy, verbatim: *"if that is not accurate enough, one
full render + ebur128 measurement at the end of convergence is the check."* That is the substance of
Q18 option (C) in §28.3: keep the model as the fast search, measure the final trim from one render so
the model's error is **corrected rather than trusted**, and re-validate against the delivery bar the
product actually has. The emit path already renders and measures once, so this does not add a render —
it re-uses the guard's.

I have **not** implemented that, because it requires the coordinator to decide whether answer 9's
0.15 LU budget is restated as "does the measured-corrected artifact clear the delivery bar" or held
hard on the model's own prediction. §28.3 states my recommendation and the consequence of each branch.

| BIG | 9000.00 | r2 | −4.10 | 2 | −16.0 | −1.3 | 0.374 @ 3600.0 | 0.0 | 4.1 | 0 | −1.3289 | 864,002,048 |

- **Worst 240 s window ≤ 0.9 LU, both families — PASS, all 7.** Worst anywhere: **0.565 LU** (56F1).
  OBSERVED that the reported figure is the both-family maximum: `model.py:174`
  `return windows(series, dur, 0.0) + windows(series, dur, OFFSET)`, `WINDOW = 240.0` (`model.py:62`),
  and `worst_win_dev` (`model.py:178`) is `max(abs(w["lufs"] - TARGET))` over that list.
- **Whole program ≤ 0.5 LU — PASS, all 7.** Worst 0.1 LU (EF4).
- **Target TP ≤ −1.0 dBTP — PASS, all 7** (`tp_target_met: true` on every cell).
- **Hard TP — PASS, all 7.** `tp_hard_ok` = `scan_peak` over the **kept** artifact → `n_over == 0` and
  emitted TP ≤ 0.0. The scan is a real full-file decode (`flow.py:79-106`, `ffmpeg -i <artifact> -vn
  -f f32le -ar 48000 -ac 2 -`, streamed in 512 KiB chunks), and its interleaved-float counts match the
  durations (BIG 864,002,048 = 9000 s × 48000 × 2 + 2048, the AAC tail). Peak anywhere: **−1.115 dBFS**
  (E43F). Caveat: `n_frames_over_0_dbfs` is `int(peak > 1.0)` — a presence flag, not a count; the
  separate `peak_max_dbfs` column is the real value and corroborates it.
- The measured TRIM correction was as small as answer 10 predicted: `measured_err` 0.000 on five cells and
  +0.100 on three (56F1, 4D36… — precisely: EF4 0.000, 56F1 +0.100, 4D36 0.000, EDU 0.000, 6A58 +0.100,
  E43F +0.100, BIG 0.000), and `extra_correction_s = 0.00` on **every** cell — the second
  model-step-and-render the work order allowed was never needed.

## 30.2 The timing cells — OBSERVED, and they fail the budget as written

Answer 10: *"incremental audio cost on-demand 30-min <= 90 s and warm BIG <= 600 s (guard included,
BELOW_NORMAL, station live)"*.

| cell | nominal (s) | extra (s) | guard (s) | total (s) | budget | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4D36 (on-demand, 1800 s) | 79.41 | 0.00 | 69.82 | **149.23** | ≤ 90 | **FAIL — 1.66×** |
| BIG (warm, 9000 s) | 369.28 | 0.00 | 539.13 | **908.41** | ≤ 600 | **FAIL — 1.51×** |

Buckets, per `flow.py:389-396`: `nominal` = `basis_raw + basis_kw + converge_r0 + measure_ride_r0 +
emit_r0 + measure_r0`; `extra` = `measure_ride2_r0`; `guard` = every `_r1_s`/`_r2_s` stage;
`total = nominal + extra + guard`. Video and the final scan are excluded (audio-only unit).

Raw stage output, quoted from `flow.log`:

```
=== 4D36 dur=1800.02 ===
  basis raw 7.97s kw 5.65s n=18000
    measure_ride_r0_s: 23.02s ...
    emit+measure r0: 32.87s + 9.02s -> I=-16.0 TP=-0.4 ...
  guard: -0.40 dBTP emitted -> re-converge at -2.40
    measure_ride_r1_s: 26.48s ...
    emit+measure r1: 34.39s + 8.02s -> I=-16.0 TP=-1.1 ...
  -> kept r1@-2.40 loud=PASS hard=PASS | audio nominal 79.41s guard 69.82s total 149.23s
=== BIG dur=9000 ===
  basis raw 43.7s kw 36.33s n=89999
    measure_ride_r0_s: 100.37s ...
    emit+measure r0: 145.93s + 36.33s -> I=-16.0 TP=0.0 ...
  guard: +0.00 dBTP emitted -> re-converge at -2.80
    measure_ride_r1_s: 83.74s ...
    emit+measure r1: 135.45s + 37.54s -> I=-16.0 TP=-0.0 ...
  guard: -0.00 dBTP emitted -> re-converge at -4.10
    measure_ride_r2_s: 86.38s ...
    emit+measure r2: 150.22s + 35.66s -> I=-16.0 TP=-1.3 ...
  guard: -1.30 dBTP emitted, no round needed
  -> kept r2@-4.10 loud=PASS hard=PASS | audio nominal 369.28s guard 539.13s total 908.41s
```

Two facts a reader must have before interpreting those numbers:

1. **The guard is not a rare path.** It fired on 5 of 7 cells (EF4, 56F1, 4D36, 6A58, BIG). On BIG it cost
   539.13 s against a 369.28 s nominal path — 59 % of the total.
2. **A 90 s budget cannot be certified from this pass, at any accounting.** The other on-demand cell
   measured here, **E43F, is 230.15 s nominal for the same 1800 s duration**, from
   `measure_ride_r0 71.03 / emit_r0 138.24 / measure_r0 6.82` against 4D36's `23.02 / 32.87 / 9.02` —
   4.2× the emit time for identical nominal work. INFERRED to be external contention — the live 3-channel
   station and, observed at exit, a peer worktree's full `pytest` run (see §30.6) — not the code. The
   panel's spread on the same-duration nominal path is **65.23 (6A58) → 230.15 (E43F) s**. INFERRED: a 90 s budget needs a station-load allowance or a per-cell rule; a
   single measurement cannot distinguish "the feature costs 79 s" from "the box was busy".
3. **The harness pays work the product may not.** `nominal` includes two full-file basis decodes
   (`basis_raw + basis_kw`, `flow.py:123,126`, cached) that exist to build the block model in this
   research harness — 13.62 s (4D36), 80.03 s (BIG). INFERRED: the product number is smaller by an
   amount this harness cannot quantify, which is why the budget could not be converted into a pass here.

## 30.3 The guard's correction law is falsified on BIG — OBSERVED, and it is the sharpest finding

`guard_ceiling_dbtp` (`loudness_ride.py:1243-1252`) steps the ceiling by `overshoot + TP_GUARD_MARGIN_DB`
(0.3 dB), i.e. it assumes the emitted true peak tracks the ceiling one-for-one. BIG, from `flow.log`
above:

| round | ceiling (dBTP) | emitted TP (dBTP) | response to the −1.30 dB ceiling step |
|---|---|---|---|
| r0 | −1.50 | **+0.00** | — |
| r1 | −2.80 | **−0.00** | **0.00 dB** |
| r2 | −4.10 | **−1.30** | −1.30 dB |

The first 1.30 dB ceiling reduction bought **nothing**; the second took effect in full. `TP_GUARD_MAX_ROUNDS
= 2` (`loudness_ride.py:1263`) permits three attempts, BIG spent **all three**, and it cleared the
≤ −1.0 dBTP target by 0.3 dB on the last one available. Mechanism, already proved by `preenc.py` in §29 of
the questions file and reproduced in the log quoted there: the limiter held to **+0.0034 dB** over its own
ceiling (encoder input max −4.2966 dBFS against a −4.30 dBFS ceiling, `n>0dBFS=0`) while the AAC round trip
added **up to +3.27 dB** over it (−1.0288 dBFS). **OBSERVED: the emitted true peak is a property of the
codec's overshoot, not of the limiter ceiling the guard is stepping — so the guard's step law and the
codec's behaviour are only loosely coupled.** INFERRED consequence: on BIG-class content the hard-TP gate
is met by the third round landing, not by construction; had r2 returned ≈ 0.0 dBTP, `guard_next_ceiling`
would return `None` (rounds exhausted) and `select_leveled_attempt` would ship the coldest
loudness-passing attempt with `target_met = False` and a warning — an artifact possibly over 0.0 dBTP
airing with a warning. Whether that is acceptable is a product decision (Q20 in the questions file).

Related, carried and now sharpened: `r5/cell7.py:61-67` sets `WARM_FLOOR_S = 300.0`,
`ON_DEMAND_BUDGET_S = 300.0`, `warm_budget_s(dur) = max(300, dur/8*1.5)` — **round 6's 300 s/300 s against
answer 10's 600 s/90 s.** I did not tune the harness to either number; both are yours to reconcile.

## 30.6 Doubts and gaps

- **The 90 s on-demand budget is unmeasured, not merely failed.** Reading (A) fails it 1.66×; reading (B)
  passes 4D36 but fails E43F; only (C) — product-path, station-live, against the old path — measures the
  thing the budget names. My numbers cannot settle it and I am not treating them as if they did.
- **Why E43F's emit took 4.2× 4D36's — contention, now with one observed corroborator.** I did not
  instrument CPU during the panel, so the cause is inferred from the stage pattern. But at exit this
  session I found, OBSERVED via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, a **concurrent pytest run belonging to the
  other worktree**: PID 10456 = `C:\Users\scott\Documents\Codex\2026-09-16\re\civiccast-ds-int\.venv\
  Scripts\python.exe -u -m pytest tests/egress tests/stream -q -p no:randomly`, with a child at PID 38836.
  INFERRED: the box was carrying a second full test suite during the panel, which is the most likely source
  of the same-duration swing. I cannot prove it overlapped E43F's window — I only know it is live now — so
  this narrows the doubt without closing it. A repeat of E43F with no peer suite running would settle it,
  and it is the reason Q19(c)'s product-path measurement should be taken alone on the box.
- **The r7-artifact divergence remains open** (§29 of this file). My replicated BIG emit carries one
  decoded sample at +0.0619 dBFS where r7's kept artifact tops out at −2.5947 dBFS, with every argv
  string matching. The mechanism found this session is AAC frame-grid alignment (700 s × 48000 = 33,600,000
  ≡ 512 mod 1024, a half-frame shift in the encoder grid), and the one unrun decisive test is a re-emit
  with the curve perturbed by ±5e-5 dB to rule the rounding knife-edge in or out. It did not block the
  panel — the panel's BIG r0 at the nominal ceiling measured TP +0.00 and the guard corrected it — but the
  product consequence stands: **the emitted true peak depends on the segment's inpoint modulo the
  1024-sample AAC frame, so the TP gate is alignment-sensitive.**
- **The guard's non-monotone response (§30.3) is a one-observation finding.** It is grounded in the codec
  overshoot `preenc.py` measured independently, but "the first 1.30 dB of ceiling bought 0.00 dB of TP" is a
  single cell's two data points, not a law. I would not generalise it beyond BIG-class content without a
  second artifact at a different ceiling.
- **`tp_hard_ok`'s scan counts a boolean.** `n_frames_over_0_dbfs` is `int(peak > 1.0)`; if the intent is a
  count of offending samples, the harness under-reports. The `peak_max_dbfs` column makes the gate
  decidable anyway, but the field name overstates what it holds.

## 31.3 The guard's one re-encode round is adverse as often as it helps, and on BIG the ceiling→TP response is adverse outright — OBSERVED

All five cells that ran a guard round, from `panel12.log`:

| cell | ceiling step | emitted TP r0 → r1 | sign | kept |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EF4 | −1.50 → −2.00 | −0.8 → −0.2 | worse | r0 |
| 56F1 | −1.50 → −2.30 | −0.5 → −1.0 | better | r1 |
| 4D36 | −1.50 → −2.40 | −0.4 → −0.2 | worse | r0 |
| 6A58 | −1.50 → −2.30 | −0.5 → −1.5 | better | r1 |
| BIG | −1.50 → −2.80 | +0.0 → +0.9 | worse | r0 |

Better on **2 of 5**, worse on **3 of 5**; the keep-best selector absorbed the three (kept round 0 each time). BIG's
three-point response — −1.50 → +0.00, −2.80 → +0.90, −4.00 → +1.10 dBTP, i.e. overshoot +1.50 / +3.70 / +5.10 dB —
is monotone in the **wrong** direction: 2.5 dB of extra ceiling bought 1.1 dB *more* emitted true peak. Round 7 had
0.00 dB of response to a −1.30 dB step, and this round the same step gives +0.90 dB. **Mechanism INFERRED, not
proved here:** the emitted true peak is the AAC codec's overshoot over an already-clamped signal, and a harder
clamp gives the codec more to overshoot.

The round also moves loudness, which decision 1's "no re-convergence, no model step" round does not model — the trim
is converged at the nominal ceiling: EF4 −16.1→−16.2, 56F1 −16.1→−16.2, 4D36 −16.0→−16.4, 6A58 −16.1→−16.2 (all
inside the window on their own artifact), **BIG −16.0→−16.6→−17.1**. So the round's side-effect on loudness is
content-dependent and reaches 1.1 LU on BIG.

Finally, **BIG's failure is upstream of the guard**: round 0, at the ordinary −1.50 dBTP nominal ceiling, emitted
+0.00 dBTP with one decoded sample at +0.0036 dBFS — both legs of the hard gate fail on the artifact the nominal
path alone would have shipped.

| EDU | 1800.12 | 152.78 | 0.00 | nominal FAIL vs the 120 s warm law; no guard round ran |

| E43F | 1800.04 | 73.02 | 0.00 | nominal PASS; no guard round ran |

## 31.7 Doubts and gaps

- **The adverse step law rests on five cells and one run.** Three of five guard rounds moved the emitted TP the
  wrong way and BIG's three points move it the wrong way monotonically, but a single panel is not a law; a second
  artifact class at different ceilings would be the test. I would not generalise beyond "the sign is not reliable",
  which is already enough to falsify decision 1's assumed 1:1 step.
- **`n_frames_over_0_dbfs` is a boolean flag, not a count** (`int(peak > 1.0)`, report §30.6), so "over0 1" means
  "at least one sample over 0 dBFS". The decisive field is `peak_max_dbfs` (+0.0036 / +0.4018 on BIG).
- **The harness's JSON type-serialised `hard_tp_ok` as the string `'False'` for BIG round 0** while round 1's is a
  bool. Nothing in the run used the serialised dict for selection (the selector reads the dataclass), so no verdict
  changed — but the JSON is not uniformly typed, and anyone re-reading it should know that.
- **The last resort cost 285.6 s of the 866 s BIG total** (`reencode_lastresort` 235.58 + `measure_lastresort`
  40.10 + `scan_lastresort` 9.02) to make the artifact worse; the panel's own guard accounting folds it into
  `guard_audio_s` 519.51 s, which is what answer 11's ≤ 240 s anchor judges.

| 6 | `__init__` | `window_leveler: WindowLeveler = level_window` |
| 7 | `__init__` | `self._window_leveler = window_leveler` |
| 8 | new methods | `_ride_conform_tmp`, `_log_ride_degraded`, `_log_ride_selection` |

| 10 | full-asset | `loudness_method = self._ride_conform_tmp(...)`, and the loudnorm measurement only when it degraded |
| 11 | full-asset | the encode wrapped in `if loudness_method != _LOUDNESS_METHOD_RIDE:` (a ridden window *is* the program) |
| 12 | full-asset | the run + partial-output cleanup |
| 13 | cache HIT | `loudness_method=_meta_loudness_method(meta)` — read back, not assumed |
| 14 | warm promotion | the comment + `loudness_method=_meta_loudness_method(self._read_cache_meta(key))` |
| 15 | bounded path | ride first, then the loudnorm measurement only if it degraded |
| 16 | bounded path | the encode wrapped in `if loudness_method != _LOUDNESS_METHOD_RIDE:`, with unlink-on-failure |
| 17 | constants | `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION = "loudnorm-v3-ride"`, the four-value comment, `_LOUDNESS_METHOD_RIDE` |
| 18 | helper | `_meta_loudness_method` |
| 19 | builder | `build_conform_source_args` now calls the one shared `canonical_video_filter(profile)` the ride's program mux also calls |

**OBSERVED — the design points that matter, quoted from the committed file:**

- `_ride_conform_tmp` tries the ride **first** on both paths and returns `None` for *any* reason the
  ride could not run there; every `None` return path unlinks `tmp_path` first, because
  `build_video_from_source_args` carries no `-y` and a leftover file would make the fallback's own
  encode fail with `File exists` instead of airing the segment.
- Cancellation is the one non-degradation: `LoudnessRideCancelledError` re-raises as
  `SourcePreparationCancelledError`, checked **before** the `LoudnessRideError` branch, because a
  cancellation *is* a `LoudnessRideError` and must never degrade into a fallback conform on a shutdown.
- `_LOUDNORM_METHOD_VERSION = "loudnorm-v3-ride"` is mixed into `_cache_key`, so every pre-U25
  conform-cache entry is unreachable and cannot be served for a window the ride would have leveled.

## II.4 The FFmpeg grammar defect the end-to-end found, and its fix (`63cafb2c`)

**OBSERVED.** `build_video_from_source_args` emitted `-ss <in> -i <source> -t <dur> -i <ride audio>`.
`-t` is an **output** option, so sitting between two `-i` operands FFmpeg bound it to the input that
follows — the ride's own audio, already exactly the window's length — and the bound silently stopped
applying to the source. Measured on a real 4 s window of an 8 s asset through the *running ride*, the
built program carried **7.000 s of video** against **3.968 s of audio**, where
`preparer.build_conform_source_args`'s single-input `-t` produced 4.021 s. `-ss` stays ahead of the
FIRST `-i`; that one is an input option on purpose, as it is in `build_conform_source_args`.

**Blast radius, OBSERVED:** the builder had exactly **one** production caller,
`SourcePreparer._ride_conform_tmp`, which arrives with the wiring in `7ddb71d6` — so the bug was
unreachable before it, and a join-in-progress airing is exactly the case that reaches it.

## II.6 Gates

**OBSERVED — lint / format / types on the committed content.** The mirrors are taken from the git

check would be measuring the checkout, not the commit.

## II.7 Environment findings that are not mine to fix

- **OBSERVED — the ride needs an FFmpeg built with soxr.** Its true-peak ceiling is 4x oversampled
  through `aresample=192000:resampler=soxr:precision=28`
  (`civiccast/egress/loudness_ride.py`, `resample_filter`). The station's shipped FFmpeg at
  `C:\Program Files\CivicCast (Native)\dependencies\ffmpeg\bin\` has it; the stock Gyan
  8.1.1-essentials build on this box's PATH does not, and fails the filter with
  `Requested resampling engine is unavailable`. On such a host the ride refuses, the preparer degrades
  to loudnorm, and the station's audio is leveled by the pre-U25 path after one WARNING. **Disposition:
  nothing to change in U25** — the degradation is by design and the station ships the capable build —
  but it is recorded in `questions\U25.md` §31 because a self-hoster who swaps in a stock FFmpeg loses
  the unit's whole capability behind a single warning line.
- **OBSERVED, unfixed, out of answer-12 scope —** the ride's failure message misattributes a *sink*
  death to "the ride's source decode". Reported, not touched: fixing it changes a user-visible string
  the answer-11 panel's log lines are quoted from.

## II.8 What I did NOT do, and what I could not verify

- **Did not stage, push, merge, tag, release, rebase, or open a PR.** `git status --porcelain` is empty
  and nothing is in the index.
- **Did not re-run any encode of the answer-11 panel**, as the order required; the re-evaluation reads
  the recorded attempts (§II.2).
- **Did not run the answer-11 timing gate.** It was the reason for the earlier STOP, and answer 12
  neither re-orders it nor asks for it. The timing budgets from answer 10 are therefore still
  unadjudicated, exactly as Part I left them.
- **Did not write a CHANGELOG or docs entry.** The order enumerated the deliverable and did not include
  one; the repo's docs rule attaches to a push, and the brief forbids pushing. **Flagged here rather
  than invented** — if the coordinator wants the unit in `CHANGELOG.md`, say so and it is a one-commit
  change.
- **Did not add `_no_ride` to the 91 pre-existing tests**, though their stand-in media now costs a
  refused ride spawn each (measured 1.36 s for one of them, with a WARNING in the log). Deliberate:
  touching 91 tests is a large diff for a test-hygiene gain, and the degradation path they now exercise
  is a path U25 added. If the coordinator prefers them pinned to the double, that is a separate unit.
- **Did not fix the misattributed ride failure message** (§II.7).
- **Did not verify the unit on the live station.** The station is read-only to me; the run path
  (`CIVICAST_GSTREAMER_RUNTIME_ROOT` and the shipped GStreamer runtime) was not exercised, so the ride
  is proven under `pytest` + a real FFmpeg, not under the packaged worker.
- **Could not make the end-to-end module run on the default PATH** — it skips by design (§II.6). The
  green run needs `PATH` to the station's FFmpeg; the station build is the one the claim is about.
- **Processes:** none started that survived — only short `ffmpeg` probes inside `pytest`, plus the
  read-only `tasklist` / `Get-CimInstance` calls. Nothing was killed; nothing of the station's was
  touched. `%TEMP%\u25` holds all scratch (`r13\reeval12.*`, `fmt3\`, `headtree\`).

## II.9 Doubts, labelled

- **INFERRED, and the biggest one:** the ride is wired and correct *as a unit*, but the timing cost of
  the ride on a real station is still whatever the answer-11 panel said, and the answer-10 timing
  budgets remain unadjudicated (§II.8). Nothing in this implementation measures it.
- **OBSERVED:** `_cache_key` mixing in `loudnorm-v3-ride` makes every pre-U25 conform-cache entry
  unreachable. Intended (a window the ride would level must not be served from a loudnorm artifact),
  but it means an upgrade re-rides every cached segment once.
- **OBSERVED:** the 4 `test_hls_relay_video_lock.py` failures I saw in an earlier run *only* appear when
  the production FFmpeg is prepended to `PATH`; they pass on the default PATH and are a PATH-ordering
  artifact of my own harness, not of the commit.

**OBSERVED vs INFERRED, briefly.** OBSERVED: the `reeval12.log` table and its five summary lines, the
commit hashes/stats/hunk counts, the 91/96/5/2 counts, the five test names, the `-t` 7.000 s vs 3.968 s
vs 4.000 s measurements, every pytest/ruff/mypy result above, the blob-SHA-verified HEAD-tree run, the
process listing, `git status` empty. INFERRED: that the panel needed no re-encode because no kept round
moved; the soxr consequence for a self-hoster; that the pre-existing tests' ride detour is acceptable.
